The counts are exact integers. The entropy densities are numerical
evaluations of their logarithms. [A1, p 6, Table III]

For positive fill, the complete ferromagnetic background determines the
ground ordering. Flipping a nonempty proper subset S from an aligned
state changes its energy by

ΔE = 2∑_(i ∈ S, j ∉ S)J_(ij) ≥ 2{|S|(N − |S|) − 2n⁻}

where n⁻ is the total number of negative pairs. At N2000 the three
positive-fill cases have n⁻ ≤ 466 and |S|(N − |S|) ≥ 1999, so this
change is strictly positive. The two globally inverted aligned states
are the complete ground family. [A1, p 5, Eq 24]

For negative fill, the nonzero corrections are positive parent edges
with ΔJ = 2. Let m₊ be their number and let c(s) count correction edges
whose endpoint spins differ. The energy becomes

$$E=-\frac{N}{2}-2m_++4c(s)+\frac{M^2}{2}$$

Correction components favor internal alignment, while the
antiferromagnetic collective term penalizes nonzero magnetization. At
N2000 all negative-fill ground states have M = 0 and lie 20 units above
the reference E_(ref) = −N/2 − 2m₊. The terminal packet prevents
simultaneous zero cut cost and zero magnetization. Exact contraction
resolves that finite-size constraint rather than assigning independent
orientations to packets after imposing M = 0. [A1, p 5, Eq 25]

Changing 400 signed-layer edges shifts the unbridged negative-fill
ground energy by 800 while leaving the minimizing multiplicity unchanged
at this size. Adding alternating bridges then locks portions of that
orientational freedom together. The exact ratio of unbridged to chain
ground multiplicity is approximately 2.075362773088 × 10²⁰. This
reduction is a property of the ground states and is not inferred from
the number of stored joint bins. [A1, pp 5-6]

An analytic residual entropy limit

Residual entropy measures how many configurations remain at the minimum
energy. Its density is the logarithm of the ground multiplicity divided
by the spin count. A commensurate sequence removes the terminal-packet
effect and makes the counting mechanism explicit. Take N = 60r with
negative fill. Each unbridged family has N/15 ferromagnetic correction
components of size 15. Aligning each component gives zero cut cost, and
zero magnetization requires half of the components up and half down.
Thus

$$\Omega_{0,\mathrm{unbridged}}(N)=\binom{N/15}{N/30}$$

In the chain, the positive bridges pair adjacent packets, while negative
bridges equal the fill and contribute no correction. There are N/30
internally aligned size-30 correction components, and half must point
each way:

$$\Omega_{0,\mathrm{chain}}(N)=\binom{N/30}{N/60}$$

These configurations minimize the nonnegative cut and magnetization
terms separately, so an excited component cannot supply another state at
the same minimum. The ground energies are −35N/6 for packet, −163N/30
for signed packet, and −11N/2 for the chain. [A1, p 6, Eqs 26-30]

The central-binomial asymptotic gives

$$\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\ln\Omega_{0,\mathrm{unbridged}}(60r)}{60r}=\frac{\ln2}{15},\qquad
\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac{\ln\Omega_{0,\mathrm{chain}}(60r)}{60r}=\frac{\ln2}{30}$$

The bridges halve the residual-entropy density because they double the
number of spins locked into one orientable component. Exact size
calculations at N120, N300, N600, N900, N1200, N1800, and N1980 agree
with the formulas. N2000 is separate: its terminal packet requires
additional cut configurations to attain M = 0, and its entropy lies
above the nearby commensurate trend. The limit is established on the
declared N = 60r family rather than estimated from that endpoint. [A1,
pp 6-7, Eq 31]

Field thresholds and soft excitations

A uniform field couples to the retained magnetization. Let e_(*)(M) be
the exact minimum full energy at fixed M. In a field H, the ground
energy is the lower envelope

E₀(H) = min_(M){e_(*)(M) − HM}

The lower convex hull of the integer points (M, e_(*)(M)) gives rational
field crossings. The positive-fill cases select M = +2000 for every
H > 0. In negative fill, M = 0 persists until the first positive
crossing: H = 3 for both unbridged families and H = 17/5 for the chain.
Each first crossing connects M = 0 and M = 10. Any additional collinear
minimizing sectors must be included when computing the degeneracy at the
crossing. [A1, p 7, Eq 32]

The first zero-field excitation is a different question. The gap Δ₁ is
the energy difference between the ground state and the next supported
energy. The ratio Ω₁/Ω₀ compares the multiplicities at those two levels.
[A1, pp 5, 8]

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Negative-fill         First gap Δ₁              Ω₁/Ω₀     First positive
  source                                                    field crossing
  --------------- ------------------ ------------------ ------------------
  Packet                          14                  5                  3

  Signed packet                   10                  2                  3

  Signed packet                    4                 67               17/5
  chain                                                 
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------

The chain has a softer lowest excitation but a higher initial
magnetization threshold. A low-energy excitation need not carry the
magnetization required to become favorable in a uniform field. The exact
data therefore resolve why the two response measures move in different
directions. [A1, pp 5, 7-8]

Exact low energy spectra and entropy resolution

The negative-fill energy decomposition also supports an exact bounded
spectrum. Local polynomials track positive-spin count and the
nonnegative cut count c. Their products retain all terms with c ≤ 100.
Since cut contributions are nonnegative, a discarded term cannot later
return to that range. At N2000 every state satisfying E ≤ E_(ref) + 400
is represented. As E₀ = E_(ref) + 20, the resulting complete energy
window is [E₀, E₀ + 380]. It contains 184 supported levels for packet,
185 for signed packet, and 189 for the chain. [A1, p 7, Eq 33]

A separate C++ arbitrary-integer convolution produced these products. At
N12 and N18, every supported level below the cutoff agrees with direct
complete-graph enumeration for all three negative-fill sources. The
N2000 ground levels agree exactly with the separate minimum/count
calculation. The spectrum inside the retained window is exact; its
construction does not approximate those coefficients. [A1, pp 7-8]

Microcanonical entropy is defined here as S(E) = ln g(E) with k_(B) = 1.
At increasing supported energies, successive entropy slopes are

$$\beta_i=\frac{S(E_{i+1})-S(E_i)}{E_{i+1}-E_i}$$

Increasing slopes identify discrete nonconcavity. The analysis also
constructs the smallest concave majorant within the window and measures
its maximum vertical excess above the entropy points. Counts remain
exact; the logarithms were evaluated with 70-digit precision. [A1, p 8,
Eq 34]

The entropy curves have appreciable fine-level oscillation. To determine
how much is tied to resolution, the analysis sums integer counts into
fixed-width bins starting at E₀, then takes logarithms and places each
value at its bin center. Only complete bins inside the known window are
used. [A1, p 8]

  Negative-fill source     Exact levels   Width 4   Width 8   Width 16
  ---------------------- -------------- --------- --------- ----------
  Packet                        7.26627   6.27352   3.21559    1.05748
  Signed packet                 3.19872   2.76719   1.64941    0.13406
  Signed packet chain           9.25413   0.01281         0          0

The entries are maximum entropy deficits below the finite-window concave
envelope. The chain has the largest deficit at individual levels, yet
its nonconcavity vanishes at widths 8 and 16 in this window. Both
unbridged families retain finite deficits under those coarse grainings.
This establishes different shell sensitivity in the three exact spectra.
A thermodynamic claim about ensemble inequivalence would additionally
require a normalized size sequence and the relevant wider spectrum; the
present calculation resolves the finite window and its stated
resolutions. [A1, p 8, Table IV]

Controlled low temperature heat capacity

The exact window is sufficient for a canonical calculation at low
temperature because its omitted contribution can be bounded. Write the
excitation energy as δE = E − E₀. The shifted window partition sum is

Z̃_(win)(β) = ∑_(E ≤ E₀ + 380)g(E)e^(−βδE)

Every N2000 energy is even, so the first omitted excitation is at least
382. The full configuration count is 2²⁰⁰⁰, while the retained ground
contribution is Ω₀. Therefore

$$\epsilon_Z=\frac{Z_{\mathrm{tail}}}{\widetilde Z_{\mathrm{win}}}\leq\frac{2^{2000}}{\Omega_0}e^{-382\beta}$$

For 0 < T ≤ 0.25 in the archived energy units, this bound is below e⁻¹⁹⁰
even for the least-degenerate chain. A conservative bound using
δE_(max) ≤ 1, 999, 000 − E₀ propagates the omitted mass into heat
capacity at order 4β²δE_(max)²ϵ_(Z); at T = 0.25 it is below e⁻¹⁵⁶ for
every negative-fill case. These bounds control the omitted states.
Numerical evaluation precision is handled separately. [A1, p 8, Eqs
35-36]

The expectation and heat capacity are evaluated from the retained counts
with 70-digit exponentials and sums:

$$\langle\delta E\rangle=\frac{\sum\delta E\,g(E)e^{-\beta\delta E}}{\widetilde Z_{\mathrm{win}}}$$

$$\frac{C}{k_B}=\beta^2\operatorname{Var}(\delta E)$$

  Negative-fill source     Total C(0.25)/k_(B)
  ---------------------- ---------------------
  Packet                       7.49644 × 10⁻²¹
  Signed packet                1.35947 × 10⁻¹⁴
  Signed packet chain           1.93020 × 10⁻³

These are total heat capacities; division by N gives the per-spin
quantity. The lowest gaps explain their separation through the leading
expression

$$\frac{C}{k_B}\sim\left(\frac{\Delta_1}{T}\right)^2\frac{\Omega_1}{\Omega_0}e^{-\Delta_1/T}$$

The chain’s gap-4 level with multiplicity ratio 67 creates a much larger
response at this temperature than the gap-10 and gap-14 levels of the
unbridged sources. The comparison comes from exact low-energy counts
with a quantified tail, rather than from output cardinality or a fitted
response curve. [A1, p 8, Table V and Eqs 37-38]

Reusing the full joint measure

The full joint output preserves more response information than either
the ground-state or low-energy reductions. For a uniform field, its
fixed-boundary partition function is

Z_(b)(β, H) = ∑_(E, M)g(E, M, b)e^(−β(E − HM)),   Z = ∑_(b)Z_(b)

It gives magnetization susceptibility χ = βVar (M) and field-dependent
heat capacity C_(H)/k_(B) = β²Var (E − HM). A specified additional
boundary energy V(b) multiplies each Z_(b) by e^(−βV(b)). Because the
source already contains its original boundary interactions, V describes
a perturbation of those interactions rather than adding their original
contribution again. [A1, p 9, Eq 40]

The joint coordinates can also define a deliberate Hamiltonian extension
with independently chosen collective and correction strengths:

$$E_{\kappa,\lambda}=\lambda E_c-\frac{\kappa}{2N}(M^2-N)$$

The correction energy is recoverable as E_(c) = E + J₀(M² − N)/2. This
permits a normalized collective term while keeping the correction term
separately controlled. Dividing the entire archived energy by N would
scale both parts and would be a different choice. Any phase comparison
must state the new couplings and size sequence. [A1, p 9, Eq 39]

The completed contribution is an exact joint construction for six
declared dense systems and a detailed physical reading of their
low-energy organization. The graph’s dense fill is retained through
magnetization; its finite correction interfaces make the counting
tractable. The output supports future uniform-field and boundary queries
at arbitrary temperature with adequate numerical precision. Arbitrary
internal site fields or individual pair changes require further
observables unless they are expressible through the retained
coordinates. The residual-entropy limits apply to the specified
commensurate families, while the N2000 spectra and response values are
exact finite-source results. [A1, pp 3, 9-10]

Chapter VI Joint State Spaces and Layered Exact Sources

The N100 construction established an important computational pattern:
retain the complete source, then exploit a decomposition that belongs to
that source. Later spin work develops that pattern in two directions. A
richer integer table retains energy, magnetization and ordered
boundaries, allowing new exact responses after the original solve. A
separate layered method recognizes collective structure inside dense
couplings and returns an exact ground energy, degeneracy and witness.
The requested mathematical object determines which of these methods is
appropriate. [V3S01,V3S05]

A joint count is an executable source object

Use the later spin convention
E(s) = −∑_(u < v)J_(uv)s_(u)s_(v) − ∑_(u)h_(u)s_(u),   s_(u) ∈ {−1, 1}
The sign convention is part of the source identity; the earlier chapters
retain their own declared Hamiltonians. Let M(s) = ∑_(u)s_(u) and let b
be the signs at an ordered list of p retained vertices. The integer
object is
g(E, M, b) = #{s : E(s) = E, M(s) = M, s|_(ports) = b}
Every entry counts labelled configurations. Summing over M recovers the
original boundary-conditioned DOS, and summing over E, M, b gives 2^(N).
Ordering the boundary is essential: opposite signs applied to two ports
depend on which source vertex receives each sign. [V3S01,V3S04]

This extra coordinate has a simple independent count identity. If a
boundary row has k_(b) positive signs, its total magnetization marginal
satisfies
$$\begin{equation}
 \sum_Eg(E,2k-N,b)=\binom{N-p}{k-k_b}\end{equation}$$
with the binomial understood as zero outside its permitted range. The
condition checks the full assignment count in each magnetization sector,
rather than merely the grand total. The completed joint solver also
checks the projection back to its scalar DOS, independent global
encodings and small-source direct comparisons. Thus the richer object
carries several independently arranged readings of the same source.
[V3S03,V3S04]

The joint table supports an exact response transformation
$$\begin{align}
 E'={}E-hM-\frac{g}{2}(M^2-N)\\
 -\sum_{i=1}^{p}\eta_i b_i\end{align}$$
Here h, g and η_(i) are exact rational request parameters. Boundary
conditioning selects rows; a fixed-M request selects magnetization
sectors. Counts remain arbitrary-precision integers, while changed
energies remain rational. Combining equal transformed energies recovers
a complete transformed DOS. No microscopic spin enumeration is required
for this reading. [V3S01]

One can also find exact ground-state crossings. For each allowed M,
retain e_(M) = min {E : g(E, M, b) > 0} after the requested boundary
selection. Under a uniform field, the ground energy is the lower
envelope of e_(M) − hM. A crossing of two supported sectors occurs at
$$\begin{equation}
 h_* = \frac{e_{M_2}-e_{M_1}}{M_2-M_1}\end{equation}$$
The reader checks all sectors at that exact rational value and returns
all coexisting magnetizations and their counts. The implementation uses
an exact convex-envelope construction, preserving ties that a rounded
field could split. [V3S04]

The retained N = 120 signed-packet-chain source with dense fill −1 gives
a concrete example. At h = 1/2 and ordered boundary
[+1, free, −1, free], the query returns ground energy −639, ground
magnetization 2 and degeneracy 15, within exactly 2¹¹⁸ permitted
configurations. These are source-coordinate energies and magnetizations.
Their physical units are supplied by the calling source map. [V3S04]

Dense completion can expose a sparse correction

For binary signs,
$$\begin{equation}
 \sum_{u<v}s_us_v=\frac{M^2-N}{2}\end{equation}$$
If J₀ is the uniform complete-graph fill, write the actual source as
that fill plus its exact correction. Its energy becomes
$$\begin{equation}
 E=E_\mathrm{corr}-J_0\frac{M^2-N}{2}\end{equation}$$
The dense collective part is now a function of a retained coordinate.
Local factorization works on the correction graph while carrying M. The
complete original dense source remains in the final energy. [V3S03]

Subtracting the fill can remove an interaction from the correction
graph: an original edge with coupling J₀ has zero correction. This can
expose independent components and repeated local operators even though
every original vertex is coupled to every other vertex. The resulting
computational route follows the source identity and the observable being
retained; visual graph density alone does not determine its cost.

For disconnected correction components, local energy/magnetization
polynomials multiply and their coefficients convolve. Repeated
components permit exact polynomial powers and balanced products. Signed
single-gateway chains require boundary messages that retain the gateway
sign. The scalar compiler has a further specialization only when the two
complete gateway-conditioned energy polynomials agree. It explicitly
checks that equality before using it. [V3S01,V3S03]

That last condition depends on the output. Two gateway rows can agree
after magnetization has been summed out and differ as joint polynomials
in energy and magnetization. Applying a scalar shortcut to the richer
object would erase the new information. The joint compiler therefore
retains both signed messages. A field-broken chain serves as a retained
control: it rejects the equality specialization and agrees with general
variable elimination. This is a useful example of a computational
identity carrying its actual domain of applicability into the
implementation. [V3S02,V3S03]

Exact contractions beyond packet families

The shared interface also accepts explicit weighted integer graphs and a
declared decomposition. Every vertex and interaction must be accounted
for, and retained ports must match their declared block positions.
Generic sources without an admitted component or chain decomposition can
use joint polynomial variable elimination. Small local tables can be
enumerated with native integer Gray-code updates; CUDA local enumeration
and CPU FLINT composition supply a separate qualified route for the
supported cases. [V3S01,V3S05]

For ordinary energy-polynomial elimination, the local interaction
contributes the nonnegative power |J|−Js_(u)s_(v), and a field
contributes |h|−hs_(u). If
B = ∑_(u < v)|J_(uv)|+∑_(u)|h_(u)|
the resulting polynomial has exponent E + B. Subtracting B recovers
signed energy. Native integer coefficients preserve counts throughout
elimination and convolution. The table schedule and energy-support span
are separate resource quantities. Neither is the formal cardinality
2^(N). [V3S05]

The generic finite-factor contraction operation extends the same idea to
explicitly listed finite domains and signed integer factors. It sums
variables outside a retained set, with no implicit probability
normalization. An isolated eliminated variable contributes its domain
cardinality. An empty factor collection is the multiplicative identity.
These details matter when a source becomes a tensor program: omitted
interactions and an empty state space have different algebraic meanings.
[V3S05]

What the completed inventory contains

The shared installation retains 120 canonical defaults and 123
source-family records through N = 120, with packet and connected
frustrated lineages distinguished. Three packet families extend through
N = 1408. Six complete signed graph constructions at each size through
1408 give 8,448 constructed graphs. Those source definitions are
catalogued separately from their solved spectra. [V3S02]

The completed dense magnetization production contains 54 cases: six each
at N = 30, 60, 96, 120, 300, 450, 600, 750, 900. The full joint
energy/magnetization/ordered-boundary production contains 24 cases: six
each at N = 120, 300, 750, 900. Its compressed joint data total
6,871,786,917 bytes. The installation retains 1,840 native mathematical
objects, including the applicable dependencies. These numbers describe
different objects and should remain separate in both discovery and
exposition. [V3S02]

The installation’s 369 shared-route checks and 381 scoped GEN4 checks
include actual CPU/CUDA equality, weighted generic elimination, rational
responses, crossing ties and portable recovery. A3D41 executes both
retained frustrated six-spin phase sources through the shared
implementation. Starbreaker recomputes the joint operator of its
inherited N100 basis, preserves the original DOS and reads ordered
opposite-sign endpoint fields. These adoptions connect the compiler to
existing applications through their actual source maps. [V3S02]

For the retained phase chart,
$$\begin{equation}
 z=\frac{s+t}{2}+\mathrm{i}\frac{s-t}{2}\end{equation}$$
the relevant source relation is E_(spin) = 2H_(phase) and M = 2∑ℜz. The
binary field therefore supplies an exact phase-alignment response in
that model. It does not add material constituents or assign a magnetic
unit. The application receives the response using the chart and energy
convention it already supplies. [V3S03]

A layered exact ground state successor

The later dense-source method, H002708, addresses sources whose complete
graph defeats small-component or low-width elimination but whose
couplings contain a small number of collective signed layers. Its
admitted decomposition is
$$\begin{equation}
 J_{uv}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_kv_{ku}v_{kv}+S_{uv},\qquad u<v\end{equation}$$
where a_(k) and S_(uv) are integers, v_(ku) ∈ {−1, 1} and S is sparse.
With M_(k) = ∑_(u)v_(ku)s_(u), the exact energy is
$$\begin{align}
 E(s)={}-\frac12\sum_k a_k(M_k^2-N)\\
 -\sum_{u<v}S_{uv}s_us_v-\sum_u h_us_u\end{align}$$
The implementation may use a floating-point eigensystem to propose the
sign vectors. It then checks the displayed integer coupling identity
over every pair. The proposal affects admission and work, while the
accepted decomposition is an exact identity of the original source.
[V3S05,V3S06,V3S07]

Frontier elimination of the sparse residual retains the signs still
needed by future residual edges and the full vector (M₁, …, M_(K)). Each
state carries four pieces: minimum residual energy, exact number of
minimizers, total represented configuration count, and a witness. These
serve different purposes. A discarded higher residual energy cannot
affect the minimum at fixed frontier and magnetizations, but its
configurations still belong in the exhaustive accounting. [V3S07]

For two contributions reaching the same state, the merge rule adds total
configuration counts in every case. It keeps the lower energy and its
minimizing count; at equal minima it adds their minimizing counts.
Joining disconnected residual components uses min-plus convolution over
magnetization vectors, multiplying counts for independent choices. Only
after all components have joined does the method add the collective
quadratic energy and compare magnetization sectors. [V3S07]

Consequently the retained table evaluates
$$\begin{equation}
 E_0=\min_{\mathbf M}\left[e_\mathrm{res}(\mathbf M)
 -\frac12\sum_k a_k(M_k^2-N)\right]\end{equation}$$
while the separate totals satisfy ∑_(M)n(M) = 2^(N). Ground degeneracy
counts labelled configurations, including ties between distinct
minimizing magnetization vectors. The returned witness is reconstructed
and its energy recomputed against the original edge list and fields.
[V3S05,V3S07]

The answer is explicitly a ground-state object: energy, degeneracy,
witness and exhaustive-accounting/decomposition certificate. The minimum
merge does not retain all excited energies, so it supplies a different
object from the full DOS or g(E, M, b). A thermal partition function
needs the corresponding full distribution or a separate justified
construction. Keeping the return schema explicit lets the application
request the object it actually needs.

Latest completed use and the computational boundary

The retained validation admits 52 of 60 planted sources at N = 10
through 16; every admitted source agrees with complete enumeration in
energy, degeneracy and witness. Small sources keep the DOS route. An
explicitly selected other method is preserved. Automatic selection tries
the layered route after variable elimination exceeds its admission.
Sources without an admitted decomposition retain a capacity result
identifying the relevant limits. [V3S06]

In the recorded Duck1.2.4 Masquerade run, both unchanged raw dense SOLVE
tasks complete through this fallback: source A at N = 360 in about 6.2
seconds and source B at N = 180 in about 4.4 seconds within their
workers. Both SELECT tasks fail on an absent mathematical-library
objects store. The exact solves and that separate store failure remain
distinct outcomes of the same recorded run. [V3S06]

Default limits of six layers, residual frontier sixteen and residual
degree eight are implementation admission settings. They describe the
retained execution route, with configurable work and memory ceilings.
The contribution is the exact decomposition and the state carried by its
dynamic program. It extends the original N100 lesson: structural
compression can preserve the entire assignment accounting while
evaluating the requested source observable with substantially less local
work. [V3S05,V3S06]

Chapter VII The Algebra of a Complete Write

Complete Write compiles signed translations on all 262,144 addresses of
ℤ₄⁹ and retains a reconstructible rank-161 reciprocal receipt.
Sequential and compiled actions are bit-identical, and the inverse
returns every address. The derivation explains how endpoint action and
retained event history remain available as distinct outputs of the same
transaction.

The historical constructions in this chapter retain the fixed 8
September 2026 SAMA source stage. Later results are identified by their
own source records.

Overview

A complete write is not merely an address increment. It is a coupled
typed transaction. An unresolved W8 source participates in a B-activated
request; X1 retains the transient response custody; a W9 record carries
the resolved target and reciprocal receipt; U coordinates completion;
both sites return to W8 while the history remains reconstructible.

The directional address is the finite group

ℤ₄⁹,   |ℤ₄⁹| = 4⁹ = 2¹⁸ = 262, 144

Each primitive signed write is a translation in one coordinate. Because
translations form a group, any program made solely from those primitives
can be compiled to one group element and applied in one bit-sliced pass.
This is an exact algebraic compilation, not a heuristic shortcut.
Sequential and compiled execution are bit-identical, and applying the
inverse translation returns every address.

Endpoint return does not erase event history. Two reciprocal
81-coordinate views split into symmetric and antisymmetric sectors. One
common constant direction is quotiented with an explicit anchor, leaving
80 symmetric plus 81 antisymmetric coordinates: a reconstructible
rank-161 receipt. The transaction also separates the resolved projection
from the conserved orthogonal remainder, supplies exact rational-complex
analytic operators, and supports a multiplicative ledger whose write
state is recoverable when the prior ledger state is invertible.

The development history is itself part of the result. The first
installation corrected a preserved module-loader failure and installed
the fast directional primitive. A later Weil fusion remained valid in
its scope. H000137 then corrected the broader description: those
surfaces did not yet expose the entire paper algebra as one composable
transaction. The complete successor added the missing U/X1 lifecycle,
common-anchor reconstruction, distinct projection and remainder,
analytic operators, composable ledgers and native multi-write
composition without editing its parents.

Opening question and conceptual picture

The governing question is:

  What exact algebra lets a reciprocal write change an address, retain
  both directional views, complete the W8-X1-W9 lifecycle, compose many
  writes into one action and recover what was written without confusing
  the final state with its history?

The conceptual transaction is

- W8 source + W8 target

- B activates request-side weld

- X1 holds transient response custody

- signed Z4^9 translation changes directional address

- W9 target record retains axis, orientation, ledger and endpoints

- U completes reciprocal exchange

- both sites return to W8

- W9 receipt and 161-coordinate history remain available

The address after completion may equal a later returned address, but the
history need not vanish. State and history are differently typed
outputs.

Typed lifecycle and the complete transaction

W8 shell

The unresolved shell is

W8 = {0, 1}⁸

so it contains

|W8| = 2⁸ = 256

shell states. A W8 site contains a shell-state identifier and a sequence
of retained W9 records. Retention does not change the site back into W9;
it lets the completed W8 endpoint carry its receipt lineage.

Activation weld

Let H_(W8)(s) be the base vector Hamiltonian at shell state s, let
ΔH_(X1)(s; a) be the transient response contribution at activation
context a, and let B ∈ {0, 1}. The weld is

H_(W9)(s; a) = H_(W8)(s) + B ΔH_(X1)(s; a)

The two activation values provide an immediate control:

B = 0  ⇒  H_(W9) = H_(W8)

B = 1  ⇒  H_(W9) = H_(W8) + ΔH_(X1)

This B is the binary activation input. It should not be confused with
the analytic difference operator B_(t) introduced later. X1 is a
transient typed contribution and custody flag, not a ninth persistent W8
bit or a matter row.

W9 record and projection

A W9 record can be written schematically as

W9 = (s, a, ρ, ℓ; q_(before), q_(after))

where s is the shell state, a ∈ {0, …, 8} is the directional axis,
ρ ∈ {−1, +1} is orientation, and ℓ is the ledger receipt. The projection

π(W9) = s

recovers the W8 shell identity without deleting the other record fields.

U X1 completion

Activation starts with two W8 sites. The source remains W8 while X1
custody is active and the target holds the transient W9 record.
Completion performs

- W8 source / X1 custody / W9 target

- append the same reciprocal receipt to both endpoint histories

- clear X1 custody

- return source and target phases to W8

The terminal equality W9 = X1 + W8 is therefore typed lifecycle
shorthand, not ordinary material addition. SAMA-D000039 develops that
distinction and its failed literal-half controls in detail.

The signed Z_4 9 address action

Nine base four coordinates

Let

q = (q₀, …, q₈),   q_(i) ∈ ℤ₄

Each base-four coordinate uses two bits. Nine coordinates therefore
occupy 18 bits and give

4⁹ = (2²)⁹ = 2¹⁸ = 262, 144

addresses. If the low bits form L ∈ {0, 1}⁹ and the high bits form
H ∈ {0, 1}⁹, then

q_(i) = L_(i) + 2H_(i)

The two nine-bit factors are a packing representation, not independent
addresses. Base-four carry couples them.

Primitive signed translation

For axis i and sign ε ∈ {−1, +1}, define

$$T_{i,\varepsilon}(q)_j=
\begin{cases}
q_i+\varepsilon\pmod4,&j=i,\\
q_j,&j\ne i.
\end{cases}$$

Composing the opposite signed translation restores the changed
coordinate,

T_(i, −ε)(T_(i, ε)(q))_(i) = q_(i) + ε − ε = q_(i) (mod  4)

while every coordinate j ≠ i is unchanged by both maps. Therefore

T_(i, ε)⁻¹ = T_(i, −ε)

For addition of two packed translations a, t ∈ ℤ₄⁹, binary low/high
vectors obey

L_(out) = L_(a) ⊕ L_(t)

H_(out) = H_(a) ⊕ H_(t) ⊕ (L_(a) ∧ L_(t))

The last term is the modulo-four carry. Removing it would split the two
512 factors and change the directional action.

Endpoint covariance

For k ∈ ℤ₄, define simultaneous endpoint reversal

G_(k)(q)_(i) = k − q_(i) (mod  4)

Then

G_(k)T_(i, ε)(q)_(i) = k − (q_(i) + ε) = (k − q_(i)) − ε = T_(i, −ε)G_(k)(q)_(i)

and all other coordinates are unchanged. Hence

G_(k)T_(i, ε) = T_(i, −ε)G_(k)

The implementation exhausts all covariance classes rather than assuming
this identity from a sampled address.

Compiling a complete signed write program

Group law reduction

Consider a program

𝒫 = ((i₁, ε₁), …, (i_(m), ε_(m)))

Because ℤ₄⁹ is abelian under coordinatewise addition, the program’s net
translation is

$$\tau(\mathcal P)
=\sum_{r=1}^m\varepsilon_re_{i_r}\pmod4$$

Sequential execution gives

T_(i_(m), ε_(m))⋯T_(i₁, ε₁)(q) = q + τ(𝒫) (mod  4)

The compiler therefore reduces the full signed-translation program to
the one group element τ(𝒫), applies one packed addition, and retains the
inverse

−τ(𝒫) (mod  4)

Exact racecar result

The benchmark uses 4,096 writes at every one of 262,144 addresses:

4, 096 ⋅ 262, 144 = 1, 073, 741, 824

logical primitive transitions. Sequential native execution took
0.989033674 seconds. Compiled execution took 0.000554226 seconds. The
observed ratio is

$$\frac{0.989033674}{0.000554226}
=1784.531387$$

All outputs were bit-identical, and the inverse returned all 262,144
addresses. This measured speedup is reported for the stated workload and
benchmark implementation. Exact endpoint compilation applies to programs
composed from the declared signed ℤ₄⁹ translations; no uniform runtime
factor follows. The compiler does not extend to application dynamics
that have not been expressed in this group action.

Reconstructible reciprocal history

Two 81 coordinate views

Let the reciprocal histories be

L = (L₁, …, L₈₁),   R = (R₁, …, R₈₁)

Form symmetric and antisymmetric coordinates

S = L + R,   A = L − R

Naively this gives 162 coordinates. A common constant shift occupies a
one-dimensional kernel. The interface retains an explicit common anchor
c = S₈₁, subtracts it from the first 80 symmetric coordinates, and
stores

H_(nc) = H_(sym)⁸⁰ ⊕ H_(anti)⁸¹

Thus the quotient has

80 + 81 = 161

coordinates while the anchor preserves the removed constant class.

Reconstruction

Given stored nonconstant symmetric coordinates S̃_(i) = S_(i) − c for
1 ≤ i ≤ 80, rebuild

S = (S̃₁ + c, …, S̃₈₀ + c, c)

Then

$$L=\frac{S+A}{2},
\qquad
R=\frac{S-A}{2}$$

Both reciprocal views are recovered exactly. A final endpoint return can
therefore coexist with a nontrivial event receipt. The quotient removes
only the shared constant redundancy; it does not discard directional
information.

Lifted dyadic example

For a reciprocal incoming pair ((s,D)) and ((-s,-D)) sharing the same
next state (s_(next), D_(next)), define

- left = ( s, D, s_next, D_next, 0, …, 0)

- right = (-s,-D, s_next, D_next, 0, …, 0).

Then

- symmetric = (0,0,2s_next,2D_next,0,…,0)

- antisymmetric = (2s,2D,0,0,0,…,0).

The common next state appears in the symmetric sector, while the
incoming branch identity remains in the antisymmetric sector. In the
p=127 reference chain, all 125 lifted receipts reconstructed their prior
and next states.

Completed mode projection and retained remainder

Let E be an exact energy vector and C ≠ 0 the completed mode. The
orthogonal projection is

$$\Pi_C(E)=C\frac{\langle C,E\rangle}{\langle C,C\rangle}$$

The resolved response is defined separately as

R_(res) = −Π_(C)(E)

while the retained orthogonal remainder is

E_(⟂) = E − Π_(C)(E)

Orthogonality follows directly:

$$\langle C,E_\perp\rangle
=\langle C,E\rangle
-\frac{\langle C,E\rangle}{\langle C,C\rangle}
 \langle C,C\rangle
=0$$

Therefore the exact norm decomposes as

∥E∥² = ∥Π_(C)(E)∥²+∥E_(⟂)∥²

Projection is idempotent, and projecting the remainder returns zero.
This is why resolved response and orthogonal remainder must remain
distinct fields.

Exact analytic operators

Let h_(L), h_(R) ∈ ℚ(i)^(n) be rational-complex reciprocal views. Define

B_(t)h = h_(L) − h_(R)

$$P_+h=\frac{h_L+h_R}{2},
\qquad
P_-h=\frac{h_L-h_R}{2}$$

The write energy is

E_(t)(h) = ∥h_(L) − h_(R)∥²

With Hermitian correlations

$$C_{LR}=\langle h_L,h_R\rangle,
\qquad
C_{RL}=\overline{C_{LR}}$$

expansion gives

E_(t)(h) = ∥h_(L)∥²+∥h_(R)∥² − C_(LR) − C_(RL)

Because 2∥P₊h∥² and 2∥P⁻h∥² separate the symmetric and antisymmetric
energies,

E_(t)(h)−∥h_(L)∥²−∥h_(R)∥² = 2∥P⁻h∥² − 2∥P₊h∥²

The executable receipt checks these identities with exact fractions
rather than float tolerances. The operator J supplies the declared
reciprocal complex structure; P₊ and P⁻ expose its symmetric and
antisymmetric views.

Multiplicative ledger and readback

For modulus M > 1, prior ledger state D_(now) and write state s, define

D_(next) = sD_(now) (mod  M)

If

gcd (D_(now), M) = 1

then D_(now)⁻¹ exists and the written state is recovered exactly:

ŝ = D_(next)D_(now)⁻¹ (mod  M) = s

Noninvertibility is a typed outcome and returns no fabricated readback.
For a program s₁, …, s_(m), composition yields

$$D_m=\left(\prod_{r=1}^ms_r\right)D_0\pmod M$$

The separate character map

χ : ℤ₄ → μ₄ = {1, i, −1, −i}

uses χ(q) = i^(q). The directional write readback is

$$\chi(q_{after})\overline{\chi(q_{before})}
=\chi(q_{after}-q_{before})$$

which recovers the signed base-four transition in the fourth roots of
unity.

Development and deviation chain

Preserved loader failure

The first H000132 execution stopped before algebra evaluation because
the Python 3.12 dynamic parent loader had not registered its module
before dataclass processing. The unused compiled binary was preserved. A
successor changed only loader registration; equations, contract,
benchmark task and performance condition remained fixed.

Valid primitive incomplete total surface

H000132 then installed the 256-state shell, packed ℤ₄⁹ action,
covariance, rank-161 quotient, completed-mode receipt and ledger
readback. H000136 fused that directional primitive into the Weil adapter
and retained prime-spacing, prime-power and W9 history channels.

Those installations were correct in scope, but calling them the complete
paper algebra omitted load-bearing layers: the two-site U/X1 lifecycle,
explicit activation, common-anchor reconstruction, separate resolved
response and remainder, B_(t), E_(t), J, P₊, P⁻, composable transactions
and native program compilation.

Complete correction and retest

H000137 installed a successor containing the entire surface while
leaving H000132 and H000136 unchanged:

- preserved primitive and fusion

- identify missing transaction layers

- freeze complete-algebra contract

- implement one typed interface

- primary execution 19/19

- independent reconstruction 14/14

- sequential/compiled bit identity

- inverse return over all addresses

This is conceptual and implementation correction without retrospective
failure inflation: the parents retain their narrower accomplishments,
and the successor owns the claim of completeness.

Read only consumer boundary

The directional Weil adapter may consume exact write state, reciprocal
history, prime-spacing and prime-power channels. Consumption does not
grant the adapter scheduler, solver or primality authority. Factor
assignment and primality assignment remain different result types. An
application also cannot omit the dynamics needed to construct its input
ledger merely because the ledger readback is exact.

At this retained source stage, SLCQ2-RZ is the default selection engine.
Complete Exact Write survives as a delegated service in the frozen,
noncurrent SLCV21R arithmetic/application base. Historical installation
pointers describe their own time; they are not current-authority
statements.

Established result boundary and forward handoff

The Complete Exact Write surface executes the complete W8/B/X1/W9/U
transaction, the coupled signed ℤ₄⁹ address action, exact inverse and
endpoint covariance, reconstructible 161-coordinate history,
completed-mode projection, analytic reciprocal operators and
multiplicative-ledger readback. Its compiler reduces any declared
signed-translation program to one exact group element and reproduces
sequential output bit for bit.

The result does not convert unrelated dynamics into translations, assign
physical meaning to X1, create scheduler authority, assign primality or
alter current Q2 selection. The history surface hands forward to
SAMA-D000042; the Prime-Ruler application boundary hands forward to
SAMA-D000056; and the current selection-plane relation hands forward to
SAMA-D000055.

Chapter VIII Lifecycle Reception and Carrier Return

The W8-X1-W9 lifecycle gives the exact program a typed request, response
and completion sequence. The corrected carrier construction uses Theta18
for normalization while retaining X1 as the response type. This chapter
follows that construction into ordered execution, reception and return,
including the controls that distinguish its source roles.

The historical constructions in this chapter retain the fixed 8
September 2026 SAMA source stage. Later results are identified by their
own source records.

Overview

The W8-X1-W9 lifecycle describes one reciprocal transition, not three
freely interchangeable objects. W8 is the unresolved eight-position
substrate state. B/contact prepares the request-side transition. X1 is
the one transient reciprocal response channel. W9 is the resolved
composite state or terminal receipt of the completed relation,

W9 = X1 + W8

The equality is typed. It cannot be read as ordinary inventory addition,
as a claim that X1 is matter, or as an equation saying B and X1 are two
scalar halves. The exact program surface can require B and X1 as
differently typed inputs and return W9 while a physical causal weld
remains open. That is not a defect in the lifecycle. It is the reason
the lifecycle is useful: syntax, execution, receipt, carrier and
physical identification can be developed without being collapsed into
one assertion.

The evidence chain is cumulative. CR120V rejected the literal two-half
entity formula while retaining a typed outward/return relay. CR120W then
preserved a failed attempt to weld the A-field carrier directly to X1.
CR120X corrected the route by using Theta18 as a carrier-normalization
quantum while keeping X1 as the response type, and CR120Y stress-tested
that separation. CR120Z and CR120ZB carried the ordered B-then-X1
grammar into exact and finite-shot quantum sandboxes, but neither
installed physical definitions of B or X1.

Opening question and conceptual picture

The governing question is:

  How can an unresolved substrate state receive contact, return
  reciprocal information and finish as a resolved write while retaining
  the identity of the request, response, carrier and receipt?

The compact picture is

- W8 unresolved source/site state

- - B/contact prepares the request ->

- X1 transient reciprocal response

- - typed resolution ->

- W9 resolved composite state and retained receipt

This picture contains two related but nonidentical descriptions.

1.  State description. W8 and W9 name the unresolved and resolved ends
    of the relation.

2.  Event description. B/contact and X1 name the differently typed
    request-side and response-side participation needed to traverse
    between those ends.

The lifecycle therefore keeps endpoint state separate from the event
that produced it. A terminal W9 receipt records that the typed route
completed; it does not turn W9 into a new independent source object.
Similarly, X1 can be required by the type system without the installed
runtime exposing an independently variable X1 payload.

Definitions domains and type boundaries

The four lifecycle objects

- W8 / S8_BINARY_SURFACE. Type in this document: unresolved binary
  substrate/site surface; Function: supplies the source-side state to be
  resolved; Explicit nonidentity: not W9; not an observed matter row.

- B / B_CONTACT_OPERATOR. Type in this document: non-scalar
  contact/request operator; Function: prepares or admits the
  request-side transition; Explicit nonidentity: not X1; not scalar 1/2.

- X1 / X1_AXIS_SELF_CHANNEL. Type in this document: axis/response
  channel with registered scalar value 1; Function: supplies the
  transient reciprocal response-side occurrence; Explicit nonidentity:
  not Theta18, matter, a row or two half-operators.

- W9 / W9_CLOSURE_WITNESS. Type in this document: closure witness and
  resolved composite; Function: records the completed typed relation
  X1+W8; Explicit nonidentity: not a third primitive object and not
  scalar-nine CARRIER9.

The base identity is

RESOLVE (W8, B, X1) → W9,   W9 = {X1 + W8}

The braces emphasize a completed relation. The expression is not a
license to remove B, exchange B and X1, or infer that two equal scalar
contributions were added.

Source row operator carrier and receipt are different domains

The lifecycle crosses several domains, each with its own counting rule:

- N100 source grammar. Counted object: 100 independent source
  rows/spins; What is not counted there: five derived W9 receipts, X1,
  non-row A operator.

- 105-row visible QP lane. Counted object: all source records remain
  visible; What is not counted there: counted/not-counted overlay does
  not delete a row.

- lifecycle runtime. Counted object: typed operator arguments and one
  result entity; What is not counted there: scalar values are not an
  information-capacity count.

- Theta carrier lane. Counted object: released carrier unit of size 18;
  What is not counted there: X1 response identity and ordinary matter
  lift.

- history/readback. Counted object: terminal and ordered event receipts;
  What is not counted there: an additional material object.

The five W9 records in the N100/RH grammar are terminal records of
completed X1+W8 relations. They remain visible for custody, but they
contribute no new physical/source row. Counting them as five more
independent objects would change the grammar from 100 to 105 rather than
describe its execution.

Non row A and the open causal seams

Volume II supplies a narrower installed provenance chain:

- A_OPERATOR: established non-row transform

- B/contact -> X1 axis fee -> W9: supported typed bridge

- A_OPERATOR -> B/contact: open

- direct A_OPERATOR -> X1: open

The A-field carrier, A_OPERATOR, its inactive historical row proxy, B,
X1 and Theta18 are distinct. Participation in one 162-ledger structure
does not fill either open arrow.

Historical language evidence and current SLC identity

G06 belongs to the preserved SAM Language v0.6 formal-simulator
evidence. It supplies frozen B prepare/request and X1 response
operators. The later SAM Language v0.7 object is a typed reflective-tape
candidate. Neither language version is the SLC described by this
retained source revision.

The immutable Volume III technical spine records the SLC authority that
was current when that spine was written. At the retained source stage,
live authority superseded that earlier pointer wording: SLCQ2-RZ is the
sole frozen/default SLC, and its byte-identical delegated
arithmetic/application base remains separately typed. This chapter uses
the historical language and Courtroom artifacts as evidence for the
lifecycle; it does not promote their version labels into the current SLC
identity.

Typed lifecycle construction from start to finish

The formal operator premise

The language-level premise separates preparation from response. In G06,
B contains a binary flip but is not exhausted by that flip, while X1 has
its own frozen response matrix. The executable checks proceed in four
stages:

1.  resolve both operators through the single formal registry;

2.  verify their frozen hashes and declared input/output shapes;

3.  apply B to every declared prepare/request column and X1 to every
    response column; and

4.  apply the involutions and recover the source basis exactly.

The hash, column, mapping and involution checks all pass. Exchanging
request and response, reducing B to its binary-flip suboperation, or
selecting an unfrozen sign/gauge would test a different operator
surface.

The typed RESOLVE gate

CR120A executes

- S8_BINARY_SURFACE : BinarySurface, Layer1StructuralCapacity

- - RESOLVE(B_CONTACT_OPERATOR, X1_AXIS_SELF_CHANNEL) ->

- W9_CLOSURE_WITNESS : ClosureWitness, Layer2Resolution

The runtime does not merely check 8+1=9. It enforces the operator
signature. The exact control sequence is:

- RESOLVE(S8, B, X1) -> W9 PASS

- PROMOTE_VOLUME(W9, D3) -> V27 PASS

- PROMOTE_VOLUME(S8, D3) TYPE_REJECT

- RESOLVE(S8, B) ARITY_REJECT

- RESOLVE(S8, B, B) TYPE_REJECT

- RESOLVE(S8, X1, X1) TYPE_REJECT

- W9 == CARRIER9 AUTHORITY_REJECT

This establishes a typed unresolved-to-resolved gate. Its secondary
boundary is equally important: the runtime does not expose an
independently perturbable X1 payload or a recoverable W9 preimage,
filling or correction certificate.

What the installed runtime computes and what it only names

CR120D opens the runtime and follows the calculation step by step:

1.  resolve the three named entities;

2.  require input types (BinarySurface, ContactOperator, AxisChannel);

3.  check authority;

4.  select the registered fixed result entity W9_CLOSURE_WITNESS;

5.  emit the operator name, inputs, result, typed-plan trace, source
    trace and warning field.

The X1 registry entry is immutable, has scalar value 1 and has no syntax
for preparing x0, preparing x1, resetting X1, or varying X1 while S8 and
B are fixed. The current RESOLVE output alphabet is the singleton

{W9}

so the installed fixed-result runtime contains zero bits of
input-dependent W9 variation. That is a runtime-contract boundary, not a
physical null experiment and not an information-capacity interpretation
of the scalar 9.

Structural continuation to V27 F81 and P80

CR120E executes the research-mode structural route

- S8 -B through X1-> W9

- W9 -> V27 -> F81

- RESERVE_CLOSURE_ADDRESS(F81, X1) -> P80

The returned P80_PARTICLE_FACE_CONTENT has value 80 and authority
STRUCTURAL_ONLY; normal mode rejects the reserve operation. The
supported structural relation is

- P80 structural row inventory

- - X1 non-row closure/contact address

- = F81 completed-face capacity.

This is narrower than a linear quotient theorem. A rank-one quotient
would require F81 and P80 vector spaces, a linear reserve map and
surjectivity. Those premises are not registered. The source-native
search also found neither a punctured 9×9 row map nor a ten-octet
grouping. The structural capacity weld therefore remains separate from a
row-to-face decoder.

Constructed split residue and mirror odd candidate

CR120G asks whether a stateful internal response can be constructed
without losing the residue. Its exact rational candidate satisfies

- parent - left - right - kappa = 0

- omit kappa = e2, scalar residual 1

- kappa_B(B0 identity) = e2

- kappa_B(B1 swaps e1/e2) = e1

- H1=[e1] stateful readout = 1

- H2=[e2] stateful readout = 0

- static memoryless control = one fixed value for both histories

The mirror model reconstructs both faces, projects its odd sector away
from a predeclared X1 fixture direction and returns rank 80. Transverse
and mixed classes are non-null with exact squared norm 5; balanced,
X1-only and degenerate classes are null. The wrong projector fails to
annihilate X1 and changes the transverse norm from 5 to 4.

This is a validated internal mathematical construction, not an installed
SAM route. No registered F81 vector space, X1 vector embedding, inner
product, loop split or 80-coordinate decoder is supplied by the
language. The model is therefore useful as a discriminating construction
while its physical and installed mapping remains open.

Worked exact parent examples

Each derived p9 record has p8 and p1 parents of the same role and
generation. For example,

$$M_9(\text{QP093A-0019})=\frac{45}{4}$$

while

$$M_8(\text{QP093A-0016})+M_1(\text{QP093A-0001})
=10+\frac54=\frac{45}{4}$$

Likewise,

$$12+\frac32=\frac{27}{2},
\qquad
1+\frac18=\frac98$$

All five linear residuals are exactly zero. Isolated parent
perturbations change the derived candidate while the direct-nine
candidate stays fixed, which supplies a dependency discriminator. The
records remain separate because downstream qA, observed-mass and
debit/credit fields can have nonzero residuals even when M_native closes
linearly.

The CR120T accounting and binding boundary

with

Scalar arithmetic does not select the source route by itself: p6+p3 can

The preserved deviation chain

First deviation the literal two half entity formula

CR120V recovers a real reciprocal half-slot grammar. The outward and
inward accounts are

$$\frac12 SW_{out}+\frac12 WRITE_{out}+\frac12 OUTSIDE=\frac32$$

$$\frac12 INSIDE+\frac12 WRITE_{in}+\frac12 SW_{in}=\frac32$$

so the complete paired route is

$$\frac32+\frac32=3=D$$

The failure was assigning this route arithmetic to two entities:

$$B=\frac12,\qquad X1=\frac12$$

B is non-scalar, X1 is an AxisChannel with scalar 1, and the shell has
six half-slots rather than two. CR120V therefore rejects the literal
entity formula while retaining the typed B-side/X1-side relay as an
archival hypothesis. Thirteen of thirteen hashes match and eight of
eight wrong controls reject; no physical intervention is executed.

mass, zero arity, a_kernel_support route, carrier_axis, non-matter
ownership and closed carrier support. The candidate ledger keeps twelve
additive modes at 81, the mirror/checksum at 81 and closure at 162.
Restoring the historical A-field proxy gives the wrong controls 82 and

A_OPERATOR -> X1_AXIS_SELF_CHANNEL edge remains uninstalled.

Ordered quantum sandbox successors

Exact minimal ordered model

CR120Z supplies a prospective minimal realization of the typed order:

- > - B:H_A - X1:CNOT_A->B -> Phi+>

The resulting Bell state has negativity 0.5, concurrence 1, fidelity 1
and CHSH 2.82842712475. B alone, X1 alone, X1-before-B and a classical
correlated-return state all have zero negativity. In the relay
extension, an X1-indexed Bell response on the middle systems heralds a
remote pair with the same negativity and CHSH values, while product
measurement and unresolved false heralds remain separable. Local
marginals stay invariant to maximum trace distance 1.11e-16.

The noise thresholds reproduce the analytic boundaries: the first grid
point with nonzero negativity is v=0.335, immediately above 1/3, and the
first frozen-setting CHSH violation is v=0.71, immediately above
1/sqrt(2). Custody closes 10/10 hashes, 9/9 hard gates and 12/12 wrong
controls.

This demonstrates quantum capability of the typed order in the sandbox.
H and CNOT are prospective maps, not installed physical definitions of B
and X1; W9 is not used as a Bell witness.
