What becomes reusable across the volumes

The two examples expose a common computational interface. A source
supplies actual ordered operands. A numerical method returns a certified
value or exact finite object. Native composition carries its
dependencies into the receiver. The final readout retains the clock and
observable to which its bounds apply. Reuse can occur at each level:
local source factors, saved joint tables, previously calculated
matrices, identical readout requests, or whole authenticated method
chains.

Volume I receives calibrated source durations, field histories and
ordered records. Volume II receives exact phase-source responses,
hidden-state retention and complete physical receiving bounds. Volume
III supplies the corresponding source objects, algebra, execution graph
and retained computations. The integration successor demonstrates a
successful method transfer with the scientific source fixed; the edge
result demonstrates an improved receiver after a specifically identified
new source calculation. Their different forms of progress now have
concrete places in one computational account.

Chapter XIX Signed Arithmetic Energy and Conditional Continuation

Retaining the original full energy makes history computable

The RH application computes exact signed Möbius source energies while
preserving their original mean, centered prefixes and metric. That
retained state supports spectral readout, signed scale transport,
explanation of excursions and finite future certificates. The attached
research paper reports 12,836 complete forcing prefixes, exact
conditional four-admission bounds at demanding states and a proved
conditional implication from a continuing source estimate to the Riemann
Hypothesis. These are distinct results with distinct scopes. [RH,
pp4-10]

For a positive dyadic scale s and a stop 0 ≤ t ≤ s, define
a_(i) = μ(s + i) for i < t and a_(i) = 0 afterward. The Möbius function
is zero on integers with a repeated prime factor and is (−1)^(k) on a
product of k distinct primes. Put M_(a) = ∑_(i)a_(i) and define the
centered prefix

$$
q_a(r)=\sum_{i<r}a_i-\frac{rM_a}{s}$$

The full source energy is

$$
Q_s(a)=\frac{M_a^2}{s}+\sum_{r=1}^{s-1}\frac{q_a(r)^2}{r(s-r)}$$

Its positive-definite kernel K_(s) has inverse Π + L_(s), where Π
projects onto constant vectors and L_(s) is the path Laplacian with edge
weights r(s − r). The constant eigenvalue of K_(s) is one; its other
eigenvalues are 1/[k(k + 1)], 1 ≤ k < s. For normalized eigenmodes
ϕ_(k), define z_(k) = ∑_(i)a_(i)ϕ_(k)(i). The full energy then has the
exact spectral representation

$$
Q_s(a)=z_0^2+\sum_{k=1}^{s-1}\frac{z_k^2}{k(k+1)}$$

Expanding in these polynomial modes preserves the original energy as a
weighted sum of squared source coefficients. [RH, pp4-5]

A truncated mode sum F_(m) through $m=\min(s-1,\lceil\sqrt s\rceil)$ has
uniform omitted energy at most one. Put N_(a) = ∑_(i)a_(i)² ≤ s. When
m < s − 1, orthonormality gives the explicit enclosure

$$
0\le Q_s(a)-F_m\le\frac{N_a-\sum_{k=0}^{m}z_k^2}{(m+1)(m+2)}\le1$$

When every mode is retained the tail is zero. This is a proved geometric
enclosure, rather than an empirical statement inferred from the observed
prefixes.

The first nonconstant mode explicitly consumes history. With
integer-normalized p₁(i) = s − 1 − 2i and signed prefixes
P_(r) = ∑_(i < r)a_(i), summation by parts gives

$$
A_1=(s-2t+1)M_a+2\sum_{r=1}^{t-1}P_r$$

At (s, t) = (8192, 6994), M_(a) = −10 and the signed prefix area is
−149,308, giving A₁ = −240, 666. Its mode contributes approximately
0.158034139, while the mean contributes 0.01220703125. The endpoint mean
alone cannot reproduce that prefix-area contribution. [RH, p5]

Signed grouping retains the cancellations in the source

The divisor calculation groups actual signed factor-pair terms into
product bands while retaining all interband crosses. At (8192,6994), the
sum of band self energies is approximately 28,252.560385784 and the
signed cross sum is −28,252.184906209; their joint retained energy is
0.375479575. At (8192,1670), the corresponding numbers are
3,340.554542529, −3,339.542843749 and 1.011698779. [RH, pp5-6]

The large cancellation is part of the exact quantity. Summing only
positive band self energies would answer a different question. The
returned records retain the original source, stop and metric so that
regrouping preserves the same joint energy. A dual calculation also
returns the exact maximizing finite test vector and a sum-of-squares
attainment certificate, making the demanding direction a readable
operand rather than the outcome of a random direction search. [RH, p5]

Signed scale transport has an explicit arithmetic forcing

At the next dyadic scale, let 1 denote an indicator and define the
stopped fine source and parity forcing by

z_(j) = μ(2s + j)1_(j < 2t)

b_(i) = [1_(s + i even)μ(s + i) + μ(2(s + i) + 1)]1_(i < t)

Pair the fine source into v_(i) = z_(2i) + z_(2i + 1) and let
d_(i) = z_(2i) − z_(2i + 1). The Möbius parity relation gives
v = −a + b. Both even and odd fine cuts are recovered from the coarse
centered cuts and the within-pair differences:

q_(z)(2r) = q_(v)(r) = −q_(a)(r) + q_(b)(r)

$$
q_z(2i+1)=\frac{q_v(i)+q_v(i+1)+d_i}{2}$$

The excess

$$
\mathcal E(s,t)=\frac12Q_s(b)-\langle a,b\rangle_s-\frac12Q_s(a)=\frac12Q_s(v)-Q_s(a)
$$

is a signed quantity. The exact full-energy transport is

Q(2s, 2t) = Q(s, t) + ℰ(s, t) + ℛ(s, t),   ℛ(s, t) ≤ 3

For an odd fine stop, the additional admission term has upper bound two.
The source supplies inherited energy, forcing self energy, signed cross
interaction and geometric remainder as parts of one reconstruction. The
bound is one-sided; it does not replace negative terms by their
magnitudes. [RH, p6]

At the next admission, write α for the parent coefficient, β for its
forcing coefficient, p = (K_(s)a)_(j), q = (K_(s)b)_(j) and
k = (K_(s))_(jj). Exact expansion yields

$$
\Delta\mathcal E=(\beta-\alpha)q-(\beta+\alpha)p+\left(\frac{\beta^2}{2}-\alpha\beta-\frac{\alpha^2}{2}\right)k$$

The weighted pairings p and q determine how the new signed coefficient
interacts with the state already present. Signs alone are insufficient.
If β = α throughout a consecutive interval, v is unchanged and the
excess telescopes as

ℰ(t) − ℰ(t₀) = Q_(s)(a_(t₀)) − Q_(s)(a_(t)) ≤ Q_(s)(a_(t₀))

This is a source-conditioned run identity valid for any length of such a
run. [RH, pp6-7]

Complete finite scans locate the excursions

  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
           Scale       Complete   Maximum excess     Maximizing Exceedances of
                       prefixes                            stop       one half
  -------------- -------------- ---------------- -------------- --------------
              32             33   0.063413987429              1              0

             512            513   0.210217371429            481              0

            4096           4097   0.514400059116           1071              5

            8192           8193   0.520439137454           7798              8

           Total         12,836                                             13
  ----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every prefix in those four listed ranges satisfies the tested ceiling
ℰ ≤ 1. The proposed half ceiling has thirteen exact counterexamples. The
separate 15,364-prefix signed-divisor gain campaign measures another
quantity and is not added to this census. [RH, pp7-8]

At scale 4096, forcing contributes approximately +0.245027 from the
stress interval’s start to the peak, while weakening inherited energy
contributes +0.005809. The excess recrosses one half at stop 1077 and
falls below the interval’s starting excess at 1130. At the scale-8192
peak admission, α = β = 1 leaves v unchanged; the excess rises because
the inherited parent energy decreases. The broader buildup is still
forcing-led. Retaining the increments distinguishes the cause of a final
peak admission from the cause of its preceding accumulated rise. [RH,
pp7-8]

The current weighted state certifies a finite continuation

The prospective certificate starts with the actual current source and
permits every future sign compatible with known squarefree support. A
squareful coefficient is zero; an unrevealed squarefree coefficient may
be either +1 or −1. The method enumerates all such branches through at
most four admissions, updates the exact weighted state on each branch,
and returns the largest attained excess and terminal drift interval. It
does not receive future nonzero Möbius signs. Relaxing their
dependencies includes every actual continuation in the admitted family.
[RH, pp7-9; H001428]

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Starting      Current excess Upper over next Terminal drift interval              Branches
  state at                     four admissions                               
  scale 4096                                                                 
  ------------ --------------- --------------- ----------------------------- ---------------
  Peak t=1071      0.514400059     0.589424759 [−0.069410267,+0.075024700]                32

  Recrossing       0.493799231     0.600499614 [−0.096696737,+0.106700383]                64
  t=1077                                                                     

  Recovery         0.261231122     0.343086070 [−0.072035796,+0.081854948]                64
  t=1130                                                                     
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

All three states certify remaining below one over that horizon. The
recovery state also certifies remaining below one half. The recrossing
state has a current value below one half but does not certify that
tighter horizon bound. The successful operation is a rigorous finite
family bound from the weighted state; it is not a forecast of which
future branch occurs. The companion frozen sign-pattern predictor failed
its proposed overshoot bounds on separate evaluation windows. Those
failures delimit the predictor, while the branch certificates retain
their different exact basis. [RH, pp7-9]

A continuing estimate has a proved arithmetic consequence

The analytic target is a uniform upper bound on the excess. If, for
every η > 0, a constant C_(η) independent of dyadic scale and stop
satisfies

ℰ(s, t) ≤ C_(η)s^(η)

then the original full energy is uniformly subpower and the Riemann
Hypothesis follows. This is a proved conditional implication. The finite
scans and four-step certificates do not supply its uniform hypothesis.
[RH, pp9-12]

To see the chain, let H_(j) = max_(t)Q(2^(j), t). Signed scale transport
and the odd-stop allowance give

H_(j + 1) ≤ H_(j) + C_(η)2^(jη) + 5

Summing that recurrence makes H_(K) subpower in 2^(K). The mean term of
the original energy bounds every stopped dyadic Möbius sum by
$\sqrt{sQ}$. Dyadic decomposition then gives the Mertens bound of order
x^(1/2 + ε) for every ε > 0. Partial summation extends the reciprocal
zeta Dirichlet series to the half-plane ℜw > 1/2, and the zeta
reflection relation completes the conditional RH conclusion. The source
estimate, the transport implication and the finite evidence remain
distinguishable at every step.

The paper also proves a flexible sufficient transfer threshold: a
smaller-source energy exponent may approach two from below at a
specified logarithmically decreasing saving and still imply the required
subpower estimate. At exactly exponent two, that recurrence permits
power-law energy. The strict saving belongs to the stated analytic
hypothesis. The computational result is that the source-preserving
system has made that hypothesis an explicit question about retained
signed operands, rather than changing the energy whenever a stricter
empirical ceiling fails. [RH, pp9-10,12]

Chapter XX Reciprocal History and the Arithmetic Interface

Reciprocal History constructs a nonbacktracking operator on the 520
directed edges of the N100 source and pairs 10,700 oriented primitive
histories into 5,350 reciprocal classes. Its corrected arithmetic
interface retains the ordinary-prime tower, coefficient-one grading and
completed signed form. The finite constructions and the all-scale
nonnegativity question are developed at their respective scopes.

The historical constructions in this chapter retain the fixed 8
September 2026 SAMA source stage. Later results are identified by their
own source records.

Overview

Reciprocal History begins with paths, not scalar state counts. On the
unchanged N100 graph, every undirected edge has two orientations. The
resulting 520 directed-edge states support a nonbacktracking transfer
operator that records ordered continuation, reversal and primitive-cycle
data. Pairing 10,700 oriented primitives with their reverses produces
5,350 reciprocal classes with exact carrier-weighted norms and power
laws. This is not the N100 DOS renamed: the DOS counts assignments by
energy, while RH retains ordered history on the same source grammar.

The stage chain contains a decisive preserved failure. P2 direct asked
whether the history object itself supplied the ordinary-prime Euler
coefficient. Its first coefficient was 100 rather than 2, so that direct
identification is falsified at its exact scope. P2A and P2B are
successors, not retrospective relabels. P2A reconstructs ordinary primes
through the radix-12 first- singularity tower n = ord_(q)(12). P2B
supplies coefficient one from the N100 role/depth grading, while N95 and
N105 controls return index zero. No nontrivial-zero ordinate chooses the
tower.

The accumulation field supplies the real coordinate that turns
multiplication into translation:

$$q=\frac{A_{total}-A_0}{1-A_0}=\frac{r_c}{r},
\qquad
u=-\log q=\log\frac r{r_c}$$

Scaling r ↦ nr becomes u ↦ u + log n, and the half-density becomes
n^(−1/2). On this coordinate the operator L = −d²/du² + 1/4 has endpoint
null modes e^(±u/2), giving the domain on which the completed
prime-power translations act.

P3 retains the completed arithmetic

$$\xi(s)=\tfrac12s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)=\xi(1-s)$$

and the paired completed Weil form. Its continuous energy, finite
centered von-Mangoldt displacement sum and floor term form one object;
splitting them into individually divergent pieces changes the
calculation.

Four authority surfaces remain distinct: the public RH claim, the
internal universal-nonnegativity assignment, optional finite F81
spectral research, and operational SLC/Starbreaker integration. The
earlier retained operational adapter is SLCQ2-RZ-RH-MOMENT-ADAPTER-V1.
The later source amendment below records Q3RHV2 as the RH-local
successor and preserves Q2 as a noncurrent exact oracle. Neither adapter
merges those questions.

Opening question and conceptual picture

The governing question is:

  How does a finite source grammar carry reciprocal path information
  into an ordinary-prime tower, a real logarithmic translation
  coordinate and the completed arithmetic form while preserving the
  exact failure and authority boundaries encountered along the way?

The chain is

- N100 source graph

- 520 directed nonbacktracking states

- primitive histories and reciprocal classes

- P2 direct obstruction retained

- P2A radix-12 prime tower + P2B coefficient-one grading

- accumulation-derived u = log(r/r_c)

- pi/Gamma completion and xi reciprocity

- completed Weil quadratic form

- separately typed public, internal, spectral and operational surfaces

No successor is allowed to erase the failed direct identification. Each
new arrow supplies a different missing structure.

The reciprocal history object

Directed edge state space

N100 has 260 undirected source edges. Orient each edge both ways. The
directed state census is therefore

2 ⋅ 260 = 520

Write a directed edge as e = (u, v). The nonbacktracking transfer B has
entries

$$B_{(u,v),(v,w)}=
\begin{cases}
1,&w\ne u,\\
0,&w=u.
\end{cases}$$

Thus a step may continue through v but may not immediately undo the edge
just traversed. Powers B^(n) count ordered nonbacktracking histories of
length n, and traces tr (B^(n)) retain closed directed history.
Primitive-cycle and determinant receipts separate repeated powers from
primitive orbit data.

Reciprocal pairing

Reversal sends

(v₀, v₁, …, v_(n)) ↦ (v_(n), v_(n − 1), …, v₀)

P1A3/P1B retain 10,700 oriented primitives. Pairing each history with
its reverse gives

$$\frac{10{,}700}{2}=5{,}350$$

reciprocal classes. The paired object retains the common contribution
and the directional difference, with integer carrier-weighted norms and
exact power laws.

Why RH is not DOS

The N100 DOS is

g(E) = #{σ : H(σ) = E}

The reciprocal-history transfer is indexed by directed edges and ordered
continuations. Two histories may end at the same state and contribute to
the same DOS sector while remaining distinct under reversal, primitive
length or ordered path. Conversely, a DOS coefficient contains no data
sufficient to reconstruct an oriented primitive. The two objects share
source provenance; they do not share type.

Executed stage chain and deviation

P0 through P1B

The foundation is cumulative:

- P0 builds the exact 520-state nonbacktracking transfer and retains
  traces, reversal, primitives and determinant receipts.

- P1A compiles tensor and Euler types.

- P1A2 keeps W9 as five derived non-counted receipts.

- P1A3/P1B pair the 10,700 oriented primitives into 5,350 reciprocal
  classes with native norms.

The five W9 receipts remain outputs of completion. They do not increase
the native source-row census.

Preserved P2 direct failure

P2 direct tested the simplest possible bridge: identify the
history-prime Euler coefficient directly with the ordinary-prime
coefficient. The first history coefficient is 100, while the target
ordinary-prime coefficient is 2. Because

100 ≠ 2

the direct identity is falsified at that scope. The mismatch is
structural, not a tolerance or serialization fault.

The coefficient mismatch rules out this direct history-prime to
ordinary-prime identification.

The correct record is

- P2 direct question

- first exact coefficient 100

- required direct coefficient 2

- direct identification falsified

- obstruction retained

Neither P2A nor P2B changes these two numbers.

P2A successor radix 12 first singularity

For an ordinary prime q ∤ 12, 12 is a unit modulo q. Its powers form a
finite subgroup of (ℤ/qℤ)^(×), so there is a least positive integer

n = ord_(q)(12)

with

12^(n) ≡ 1 (mod  q)

Minimality places q in the first cyclotomic singularity:

q ∣ Φ_(n)(12)

subject to the exact prime-power/order bookkeeping of the tower. P2A
uses this first block order to reconstruct ordinary-prime placement. It
adds a radix-12 arithmetic route that P2 direct lacked.

P2B successor source grading

P2B uses the N100 role/depth grading

C₀|C₁|C₂ = 20|24|5

H₀|H₁|H₂ = 8|7|0

to supply the compiled family index 1. The source-native N95 and N105
controls return index 0. This distinguishes the selected N100 grading
from nearby row- count perturbations without consulting any
nontrivial-zero ordinate.

The successor logic is

- P2A: ordinary prime q -> first radix-12 order ord_q(12)

- P2B: N100 role/depth grading -> coefficient one

- controls: N95 and N105 -> zero

Accumulation to logarithmic translation

Field coordinate

The accumulation relation is

$$q=\frac{A_{total}-A_0}{1-A_0}=\frac{r_c}{r}$$

Taking the negative logarithm gives

$$u=-\log q
=-\log\frac{r_c}{r}
=\log\frac r{r_c}$$

If the radial ratio is multiplied by n > 0,

$$r\mapsto nr
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
u\mapsto\log\frac{nr}{r_c}=u+\log n$$

Multiplicative arithmetic has become additive translation.

Half density

The symmetric translation difference acts at ±t/2. For t = log n, the
decaying half-density factor is

e^(−t/2) = e^(−log (n)/2) = n^(−1/2)

This derives the prime-power weight from the logarithmic displacement
rather than inserting it as a separate numerical convention.

Endpoint operator

Define

$$L=-\frac{d^2}{du^2}+\frac14$$

For f_(±)(u) = e^(±u/2),

$$f_\pm''=\frac14f_\pm$$

so

$$Lf_\pm=-\frac14f_\pm+\frac14f_\pm=0$$

The two endpoint null modes generate the two-moment boundary. The
declared test domain is

𝒟₀ = L(C_(c)^(∞)(ℝ))

which keeps compact support and endpoint annihilation visible.

Accumulation supplies this coordinate route. It is not identified with
the N100 DOS, a local P5A symbol or a numerical energy coefficient.

Completed arithmetic

Lambda and xi

Start from

Λ(s) = π^(−s/2)Γ(s/2)ζ(s)

The completed entire function is

$$\xi(s)=\frac12s(s-1)\Lambda(s)$$

The π/Gamma factor supplies the archimedean completion, while s(s − 1)
handles the pole bookkeeping. The resulting reciprocity is

ξ(s) = ξ(1 − s)

Prime powers enter through the von Mangoldt weight Λ(n), not only
through primes at exponent one.

Translation energy

Let

(T_(a)h)(u) = h(u + a)

Define the reciprocal difference

B_(t)h = T_(t/2)h − T_(−t/2)h

and its nonnegative route energy

E_(t)(h) = ∥B_(t)h∥²

The construction is direction-sensitive before the norm and reciprocal
after pairing.

Completed Weil form

The compiled form is

$$\begin{aligned}
Q_\xi(h)=\int_0^\infty
\frac{e^{-t/2}}{1-e^{-2t}}E_t(h)\,dt\\
\quad+\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\left[E_{\log n}(h)-2\|h\|^2\right]\\
\quad-\kappa_0\|h\|^2\end{aligned}$$

where

$$\kappa_0
=\gamma-\log(3A_0)+\frac\pi2+\log2$$

For compactly supported h, T_(t/2)h and T_(−t/2)h have disjoint support
once t exceeds the support diameter. Then

E_(t)(h) = 2∥h∥²

so the centered summand vanishes. The prime-power sum is therefore
finite on each compactly supported input.

The continuous route-energy integral, centered arithmetic sum and floor
subtraction are one completed object. Separating them into divergent
pieces would change the domain and value of the form.

with equality controlled by common minimizers and strictness by kernel

Chapter XXI Signed Arithmetic Sources and Transition Storage

From the arithmetic interface to an executable source

The reciprocal-history construction in Chapter XX retains the path

makes its signed-source calculations more explicit and less expensive.
It supplies exact interval-prefix evaluation, common-grid transport, an
ordered Gram forest and a source-specific transition storage. Each
construction retains the original coefficients, their quadratures and
the terms that have already been paid.

This chapter is a Codex editorial synthesis of completed mathematical
and computational records. Sean Brady is originator and conceptual

Exact range queries preserve the whole signed recipient

One completed Type II construction uses
$$u(n)=
 \begin{cases}
 \mu(n),&n\text{ odd},\\
 0,&n\text{ even},
 \end{cases}
 \qquad \kappa=\pi/\log2$$
At scale a, put U = V = ⌊a^(1/4)⌋ and Z = a^(7/8). For fixed source
index m and odd k, the admissible c values in the original near-lattice
sum lie in one exact interval:
$$\begin{gathered}
 c\leq V,\qquad
 c>\max\{Z/m,V/k,a/(mk)\},\\
 c<2a/(mk).
\end{gathered}$$
The strict product boundaries remain in the integer admission. Define
three signed prefixes
$$\begin{aligned}
 P(t)=\sum_{c\leq t}u(c)c^{i\kappa},\\
 S(t)=\sum_{c\leq t}u(c)/c,\\
 \Theta(t)=\sum_{c\leq t}u(c)\theta_{V,c}\end{aligned}$$
The lattice part becomes one difference of P values, with its original
outside phase. The other two tables retain the continuous and boundary
compensation. The complete recipient remains
$$F=E_\mathrm{near}
 -\frac{VS(V)}2\{A(a/V)+(a/V)T(a/V)\}$$
The same signed sum contains the lattice, correction and mean.
Shared-prime pairs, source zeros and small-prefix mean terms survive the
rearrangement. [V3A01] [V3A02]

For rational a = A/D, all admission endpoints can be chosen by integer
arithmetic. With n_(Z) = ⌊a^(7/8)⌋, the interval is
$$\begin{aligned}
 L=1+\max\{\lfloor n_Z/m\rfloor,
           \lfloor V/k\rfloor,
           \lfloor A/(Dmk)\rfloor\},\\
 H=\min\{V,\lfloor(2A-1)/(Dmk)\rfloor\}\end{aligned}$$
An empty interval contributes exactly zero. Even and squareful source
zeros can be skipped, and strict ck > V excludes k = 1. There are
O(a^(3/4)) source m indices and O(a^(1/8)) odd k indices. The resulting
O(a^(7/8)) query count describes computational work. The size of the
actual signed amplitude remains its separate arithmetic question.

The completed production includes 22 distinct fresh pod windows from
4, 194, 301.875 to 40, 960, 000.125, with a preserved duplicate
execution and a corrective replacement window. At a = 16, 777, 216.125,
the directed complete value is approximately −137.312426025; at
16, 777, 216.375 it is −137.3124361512. The fractional pair retains the
moving boundary at V = 64. Every GPU discovery value receives a complete
independent 256-bit native evaluation. The exact prefix construction
preserves the moving fractional boundary, and independent 256-bit

A common grid separates arithmetic from geometric payments

The common-grid continuation groups fine odd coordinates
n = 4p + 1, 4p + 3. Its zeroth and first moments are
$$\begin{aligned}
 d_0(p)=\{u(4p+1)+u(4p+3)\}/4,\\

Both fine and coarse responses use the same moving A through 8A
coordinate grid. The retained kernel is
$$W_A(p,t)=\int_A^{2A}
       w(p/a)w(t/a)\,\frac{da}{a^3}$$
With $q=1/\sqrt2$, the leading recipient is
$$\begin{aligned}
 Q_{00}(A)={}\sum_{p\ne t}
 [d_0(p)d_0(t)-q\,u(p)u(t)]W_A(p,t)\\
 +\frac18\sum_pu(4p+1)u(4p+3)W_A(p,p)\end{aligned}$$
Its first line contains the four actual progression-pair products

The complete source identity is

 E(4A)-qE(A)
   =Q_{00,\mathrm{cent}}(A)+C_\mathrm{actual}(A)\\

where

Geometry, first moments and literal self transport have explicit

The displayed constants are outward-rounded source budgets. Their

An ordered Gram forest keeps both quadratures

A second computational line acts directly on the original weighted
source. Put s = 1 + ε, c_(n) = u(n)n^(ik), k = π/log 2, and
$$L_Y(n)=
 \begin{cases}
 [n^{-s}-\min(Y,2n)^{-s}]/s,&n<Y,\\
 0,&n\geq Y.
 \end{cases}$$
For each group having the same already-processed smooth factor, retain
its ordered aggregate
$$\begin{aligned}
 C=\sum_nc_n,\qquad W=\sum_nc_nL_Y(n),\\
 \mathcal{E}=\sum_{m,n}c_m\overline{c_n}
                  L_Y(\max(m,n))\end{aligned}$$
For consecutive ordered groups A, B, the exact combine is
$$\begin{aligned}
 C_{AB}=C_A+C_B,\qquad W_{AB}=W_A+W_B,\\
 \mathcal{E}_{AB}=\mathcal{E}_A+\mathcal{E}_B
                    +2\Re(C_A\overline{W_B})\end{aligned}$$
The cross term is part of the full energy. An AVL forest stores this
associative ordered receiver, with genuine deletions and sorted-median
construction of outgoing groups. Each physical node retains its original
integer address, coefficient, phase and kernel. [V3A05] [V3A06]

Preindexed distinct-prime events move a node from group h to hp. Only
affected roots change. Complete outgoing self and surviving boundary
energy remain separate. If S is the number of nonzero coordinates and
A_(inc) their distinct-prime incidences, aggregate/control work is
O((S + A_(inc))log S + π(N))
Source generation, coefficient admission, precision-dependent arithmetic
and retention have their own costs.

At N = 8192, the original source has 3321 nonzero nodes and 6363 prime
incidences. Every incidence moves once. The observed maximum tree height
is twelve; after the final cut every group is a singleton. Matched
complete-cut arithmetic takes 0.177855 seconds, compared with 9.930477
seconds for the complete-group baseline, a measured ratio 55.83. The
baseline already omits costly full-group serialization, so this is an
arithmetic comparison. Fourteen retained cases at 512 bits agree across
764 complete cuts; the full campaign retains 102,044 checks and 25
cold-exact native results. The retained complete-cut values agree with
the reference while the measured arithmetic work is reduced. [V3A05]

Paid subaccounts locate the cross prime task

The tree also exposes an exact source split. For a complete prime cut,
let S_(p) be self energy, B_(p) the surviving boundary energy, and
X_(p) = M_(p⁻) − B_(p) − S_(p)
The literal squarefree source cocycle gives
$$X_p=-2\cos(k\log p)D_p+R_p,\qquad
 D_p=\sum_{\substack{n<Y\text{ odd squarefree}\\p\mid n}}L_Y(n)$$
The matched prime-ratio term D_(p) has an exact all-scale Euler-product
payment. At ε = 1/2, an elementary upper is $16a/(9\sqrt3)$,
a = 1 − 2^(−s). The original off-diagonal overlap R_(p) is retained
after that payment.

The independent inequality
$$x-\log(1+x)\geq
 \frac{x^2}{2\max(1,1+x)},\qquad x>-1$$
supplies a source-level quadratic credit. With
$$Q_p=\frac{(S_p+X_p)^2}
                 {2\max(B_p,M_{p^-})}$$
the completed sharp and adaptive receivers have their explicit

 =-m|a-\ell/m|^2+|\ell|^2/m\end{aligned}$$

Chapter XXII Prime Coordinates and Exact Mersenne Factors

The prime-coordinate program constructs exact factor opportunities and
verifies the resulting divisibility certificates. Its structural
scheduler finds 103 factors against 100 at equal budget on the retained
comparison, and its frontier deployment assigns 1,282 exact factors
across 5,390 prime exponents. This chapter derives the coordinates,
compiler and evidence that support those results; a surviving finite
search row keeps its primality status unassigned.

The historical constructions in this chapter retain the fixed 8
September 2026 SAMA source stage. Later results are identified by their
own source records.

Overview

The Mersenne application begins from an owner-defined change of
coordinate: prime marks are informational radix coordinates on a closed
two-dimensional sphere, not equally spaced marks on an ordinary integer
ruler. Exact prime integers remain indispensable. They generate the
marks, define operator composition and carry every modular certificate.
The conceptual change is in the role assigned to a prime, not in its
arithmetic identity.

The rational stereographic chart

$$x=\tan(\chi/2),
\qquad
S(x)=\left(\frac{2x}{1+x^2},\frac{1-x^2}{1+x^2}\right)$$

turns multiplication into an exact sphere action T_(n)(x) = nx,
inversion into J(x) = 1/x, and logarithmic displacement into
du = dx/x = dχ/sin χ. Prime-only marks p⁻ → p form reversible adjacent
cells. On each cell the program places three reciprocal voices:

$$\alpha=2+\sqrt3,
\qquad
\beta_p=\frac{p+\sqrt3}{p-\sqrt3},
\qquad
\gamma_p=\beta_p\beta_{p^-}^{-1}$$

Repeated squaring generates dyadic pulses. A contact at depths i, j has
shallow depth s = min (i, j) and delay d = |i − j|. Transport into the
shallow frame produces an exact echo E satisfying

E^(2^(s)) = 1 (mod  q),   ord_(q)(E) ∣ 2^(s)

The executed MP-S0-MP-S4B lineage compiles those objects into ECHO16 and
exposes the genuine Mersenne factor coordinate

k = b2^(e),   q = 2kp + 1

A prime q is assigned as an exact factor of M_(p) = 2^(p) − 1 only
through the order certificate ord_(q)(2) = p. MP-S5’s sealed structural
k-order returns 103 factored objects against 100 for equal-budget
ascending counting in the retained application-scheduler comparison.
MP-S6 assigns 1,282 exact factors among 5,390 exponents. MP-S7 and MP-S8
preserve a 1,858-row roster after official-factor removal and complete
9,595-coordinate shell misses. Those misses remain PRIMALITY_UNASSIGNED.

The clean-sheet route is a successor to two preserved failures. M4’s
13,818 RH prime-sidecar ranking and M4A’s 7,708 candidate-local
Theta18/W9 ranking both fail their fixed direct-successor questions.
Neither candidate stably replaced the fixed reference scheduler. The
clean-sheet compiler preserves both outcomes.

The owner has resumed only the bounded CEV1/MP successor and its initial
training. All other production, farm, frontier and publication work
remains paused. This chapter records the installed CEV1 and MPV2
successors without launching arithmetic, remote work, promotion or
distribution.

Opening question and conceptual picture

The governing question is:

  Can the prime-coordinate, reciprocal-history and dyadic structures be
  compiled into an exact ordering of genuine Mersenne factor
  coordinates, while keeping factor assignment, PRP status and
  Mersenne-primality certification strictly distinct?

The complete route is

- prime integer p and predecessor p^-

- prime-only reversible sphere cell

- alpha, beta_p and gamma_p reciprocal voices

- dyadic pulse depths and delayed echo

- structural k = b*2^e coordinate

- prime q = 2kp + 1

- exact modular order certificate

- factor assigned OR finite miss remains PRIMALITY_UNASSIGNED

This is an application compiler, not a universal replacement for the SLC
substrate, Q2 selector, Reciprocal History, RH authority or Lucas-Lehmer
certificate lane.

Informational radix with exact arithmetic custody

The owner defined role

The owner interpretation is:

  Prime numbers are informational radix coordinates used to express
  orbit on a closed two-dimensional sphere.

The formalization retains two layers:

- arithmetic encoding/certificate. Meaning: p and q are exact certified
  prime integers; modular powers and divisibility are exact.

- SAM object role. Meaning: each prime emits one indecomposable mark and
  multiplicative sphere action.

Uniform integer ordinal is not the ruler. Composite integers still
appear inside arithmetic values such as n, k and (q-1); they simply do
not emit prime-ruler marks of their own.

Orbit and harmony are typed terms

Orbit denotes the discrete generated family

z, z², z⁴, …, z^(2^(n))

on the exact information-state surface. It is not a claim of physical
motion around a sphere. Harmony denotes exact equality, reciprocal
identity or modular closure between separately generated voices. It is
not an acoustic analogy substituted for calculation.

Closed sphere and prime only ruler

Rational sphere chart

Set x = tan (χ/2). The point

$$S(x)=\left(X(x),Z(x)\right)
=\left(\frac{2x}{1+x^2},\frac{1-x^2}{1+x^2}\right)$$

lies on the unit circle section of the closed two-dimensional sphere
because

$$X(x)^2+Z(x)^2
=\frac{4x^2+(1-x^2)^2}{(1+x^2)^2}
=\frac{1+2x^2+x^4}{(1+x^2)^2}
=1$$

The two limits x = 0 and x = ∞ close the chart at reciprocal poles.

Multiplicative actions and reversal

Define

T_(n)(x) = nx

Then operator composition is exactly integer multiplication:

(T_(m) ∘ T_(n))(x) = m(nx) = (mn)x = T_(mn)(x)

With J(x) = x⁻¹,

$$(J\circ T_n\circ J)(x)
=J\!\left(\frac n x\right)
=\frac x n
=T_{1/n}(x)$$

Thus reversal conjugates expansion by n to contraction by 1/n.

Differentiating x = tan (χ/2) gives

$$\frac{dx}{x}
=\frac{\tfrac12\sec^2(\chi/2)}{\tan(\chi/2)}d\chi
=\frac{d\chi}{2\sin(\chi/2)\cos(\chi/2)}
=\frac{d\chi}{\sin\chi}$$

Therefore

$$du=\frac{dx}{x}=\frac{d\chi}{\sin\chi}$$

The logarithmic coordinate u = log x converts T_(n) into translation by
log n, joining the sphere chart to the logarithmic translation language
of Reciprocal History without merging their application authorities.

Marks and adjacent cells

MP-S0B emits one object for each prime p. A mark retains:

- the exact integer p and reciprocal point 1/p;

- indecomposable action T_(p);

- the two exact sphere points S(p) and S(1/p); and

- the adjacent arithmetic ports (p-1) and (p+1).

If p⁻ is the preceding prime, the directed adjacency p⁻ → p defines one
reversible cell with scale

$$\rho_p=\frac p{p^-},
\qquad
\rho_p^{-1}=\frac{p^-}{p},
\qquad
\Delta u_p=\log\frac p{p^-}$$

Its exact spherical chord is computed from the two rational sphere
points. MP-S0A/S0B install 172 prime-only marks and 171 adjacent-prime
cells. No composite between consecutive marks becomes an extra ruler
object.

Three reciprocal voices

Native Lucas Lehmer voice

Let

$$\alpha=2+\sqrt3,
\qquad
\alpha^{-1}=2-\sqrt3,
\qquad
\alpha\alpha^{-1}=1$$

Modulo M_(p) = 2^(p) − 1, define

$$L_i=\alpha^{2^i}=a_i+b_i\sqrt3$$

The reciprocal trace is

s_(i) = L_(i) + L_(i)⁻¹ = 2a_(i)

Because L_(i + 1) = L_(i)²,

s_(i + 1) = L_(i)² + L_(i)⁻² = (L_(i) + L_(i)⁻¹)² − 2 = s_(i)² − 2 (mod  M_(p))

At i = 0,

s₀ = α + α⁻¹ = 4

so the common reciprocal mode is exactly the Lucas-Lehmer recurrence.
MP-S0 reconstructs this mode for all 171 prime exponents through 1024.

Candidate prime mark voice

The Cayley lift

$$\beta_p=\frac{p+\sqrt3}{p-\sqrt3}$$

has reciprocal conjugate

$$\beta_p^{-1}=\frac{p-\sqrt3}{p+\sqrt3}$$

Its norm is one, so its dyadic family

R_(j) = β_(p)^(2^(j))

lives on the same reciprocal quadratic-unit surface as the α family. The
prime integer selects the voice; the modular and quadratic arithmetic
retains exact custody.

Incoming transition beat

The transition from the previous prime cell is

γ_(p) = β_(p)β_(p⁻)⁻¹

This object records change between consecutive prime voices rather than
a second copy of the current mark. Its inverse reverses the cell:

γ_(p)⁻¹ = β_(p⁻)β_(p)⁻¹

The three families answer different questions: α carries the native
Mersenne recurrence, β_(p) carries the current prime mark and γ_(p)
carries the adjacent-prime transition.

Beats within beats

Dyadic depth and delay

Repeated squaring of any voice produces pulses at depths

z^(2⁰), z^(2¹), …, z^(2^(n))

For a contact between depth i in one voice and depth j in another, set

s = min (i, j),   d = |i − j|

If, for example, j = i + d, then

z^(2^(j)) = (z^(2^(i)))^(2^(d))

The exponent 2^(d) transports the deeper pulse into the shallower frame.
The residual relation is the base echo E. Every retained MP-S2 and MP-S3
factor component satisfies

E^(2^(s)) = 1 (mod  q),   ord_(q)(E) ∣ 2^(s)

The echo is therefore an exact finite-order modular object with a dyadic
closure depth, not a visual correlation.

Nested compiler

Factor the Mersenne multiplier coordinate as

k = b2^(e)

where b is odd and e = v₂(k). The structural base b and dyadic scale
2^(e) echo the prime-cell and pulse decomposition. The complete compiled
route is

- adjacent-prime cell

- alpha/beta/gamma reciprocal relation

- shallow depth s and echo delay d

- base echo closure

- odd structural base b and dyadic scale 2^(e)

- k = b*2^e

- q = 2kp + 1

This is the exact content of beats within beats: nested coordinate
relations are compiled into a candidate factor address that the modular
lane can certify or reject.

Exact Mersenne factor certificate

Deriving the candidate form

Let p be an odd prime and suppose prime q divides

M_(p) = 2^(p) − 1

Then

2^(p) ≡ 1 (mod  q)

The multiplicative order r = ord_(q)(2) divides p. For a nontrivial
Mersenne factor, r ≠ 1; since p is prime, r = p. Fermat’s theorem also
gives r ∣ q − 1, hence

p ∣ q − 1

Because q is odd, (q-1) is even, so for some positive integer k,

q − 1 = 2kp,   q = 2kp + 1

Conversely, if the candidate q = 2kp + 1 is prime and the exact order
check returns

ord_(q)(2) = p

then 2^(p) ≡ 1 (mod  q) and therefore q ∣ M_(p). The implementation may
certify the modular closure with pow(2,p,q)=1 together with the declared
prime/order conditions. It never needs to materialize the enormous
integer 2^(p) − 1.

Result type boundary

- prime q, candidate form, exact order p. Authorized type: exact factor
  of M_(p).

- finite k- or shell search finds no factor. Authorized type:
  PRIMALITY_UNASSIGNED.

- PRP result with its certificate. Authorized type: production
  PRP/certificate status only.

- exact Lucas-Lehmer terminal. Authorized type: Mersenne-primality
  baseline/confirmation status.

A finite miss never crosses rows in this table. It is not a prime,
probable prime or verified Mersenne-prime assignment.

Executed discovery to compiler chain

MP-S0 installs the α reciprocal common mode on 171 prime exponents
through 1024. MP-S0A installs the exact sphere actions. MP-S0B emits 172
prime-only marks and 171 reversible cells. These stages define the
object language before evaluating factor-search advantage.

- MP-S1. Exact observation: self-harmony exposes factors for 52/158
  composites and 0/13 exact positives.

- MP-S2. Exact observation: α/β_(p) harmony adds five factors beyond
  MP-S1; all 37 contacts are phase-shifted echoes.

- MP-S3. Exact observation: α/γ_(p) adds four held-out factors; all four
  are delayed echoes.

The zero counts on exact-positive controls remain part of the record.
The factor contacts motivated compilation because they closed under an
exact echo identity, not because every composite was detected.

Compilation and blind range anatomy MP S4A MP S4B

MP-S4A compiles the three voices into candidate-local deterministic
ECHO16 with checkpoints. MP-S4B applies it to a blind range and factors
50/136 composites and 0/1 exact prime. Inspecting the exact factor
anatomy exposes q = 2kp + 1, turning the voice/echo structure into the
genuine factor-coordinate interface. The blind-range result establishes
the exact candidate-local factor-coordinate interface. ECHO16 is a
language and local compiler at this point, not yet an established global
ordering.

Structural scheduler MP S5

MP-S5 seals a structural ordering of k = b2^(e) before comparison. At
equal budget it factors 103 objects versus 100 under ascending k
counting and reaches shared contacts earlier. The sealed structural
ordering therefore finds three additional factors at equal budget in
this comparison.

The result concerns this Mersenne factor-scheduling application. It does
not promote the structural ruler to the global Q2, Starbreaker or RH
scheduler.

Frontier deployment MP S6 through MP S8

MP-S6 deploys the structural ruler on 5,390 prime exponents:

5, 390 = 1, 282 + 4, 108

It assigns 1,282 exact factors without constructing M_(p); 4,108 rows
remain campaign survivors. Every assigned factor is exact; the 4,108
survivors retain the campaign’s finite-search status.

MP-S7 removes 2,250 survivors by independently verified official
factors:

4, 108 − 2, 250 = 1, 858

MP-S8 executes every one of 9,595 first-singularity-shell coordinates
for each of those 1,858 rows without assigning a factor. The 1,858-row
roster is the immutable structural freeze, and every row remains
PRIMALITY_UNASSIGNED at that boundary.

A later dated status triage yields a 1,204-row operational distribution
derivative after removing 647 distinct verified PRP composites, routing

That derivative does not rewrite the 1,858-row MP-S8 result.

Preserved direct ranking failures and wrong controls

M4 joined 13,818 ordinary-prime spacing sidecars to the completed

so M3E remained selected.

The fixed direct-ranking comparison therefore does not improve the
selected M3E scheduler.

window. That was the wrong object. The corrected source-visible bridge

- static U63 + dynamic U63

- theta18_cross_hex

- W9HistoryMode

- nine forward + nine reverse Z4 maps

M4A then evaluated all 7,708 q30 survivors with exact reciprocal,

$$\begin{aligned}
q(p)=\frac1p, A_{lift}(p)=\frac{1-A_0}{p},\\
A_{total}(p)=A_0+A_{lift}(p),u(p)=\log p\end{aligned}$$

the weight $\log(p)/\sqrt p$ and the declared pi-bearing floor constant.
The fixed candidate-local channel did not stably replace M3E. The exact
reciprocal checks pass, but the fixed candidate-local ranking does not
stably replace M3E on its declared comparison.

Its receipt is typed
CANDIDATE_LOCAL_COMPLETED_CHANNEL_RECEIPT_NOT_FULL_Q_XI. It neither
evaluates the full completed RH form nor changes RH

The clean-sheet route asks a different executable question. Instead of

orders genuine trial-factor coordinates and retains exact modular factor
decisions. This successor does not revise M4 or M4A. It preserves them
as the deviation chain that redirected the application toward an
operationally exact target.

Wrong controls for the clean-sheet route include:

  surface;

  exact information-state chart;

- replacing Lucas-Lehmer certification with the structural filter.

process is running. The completed certificate-DAG and reconstruction
results remain available independently of that execution-custody join.

For p = 143064041, k = 320000 would form

q = 2(320000)(143064041) + 1 = 91560986240001

a 47-bit candidate coordinate. The active run did not use that gate. The
calculation records a possible address only; it is not an executed
factor result, and a miss at any finite gate would remain
primality-unassigned.

The paused frontier-prime objective below ten minutes remains unfinished
in the Lucas-Lehmer certificate lane. This statement preserves the live
boundary without weakening any completed MP-S0-MP-S8 result.

Established result and forward boundary

This chapter establishes a continuous conceptual-to-technical chain:

1.  exact prime integers emit informational sphere marks;

2.  adjacent marks carry reciprocal α, β_(p) and γ_(p) voices;

3.  dyadic voice contacts yield finite-order delayed echoes;

4.  the echo anatomy factors k = b2^(e) and forms q = 2kp + 1;

5.  exact order p assigns a genuine factor of M_(p);

6.  the structural ruler supplies an application-specific search order;
    and

7.  finite misses remain PRIMALITY_UNASSIGNED.

The installed application results and their exact classifications are
retained stage by stage. No further computational step follows from this
document while the owner pause is active. Any successor requires fresh
owner authority and a new campaign that preserves frozen artifacts,
exact status types and the untouched active-remote boundary.

Chapter XXIII Reusable Mersenne Certificates and Arithmetic Compilers

A certificate is a reusable mathematical object

Chapter XXII develops the prime-coordinate and exact factor interface.
The later computational work improves how exact evidence is constructed,

certificate dependencies and source-bound arithmetic. A factor interval,
a recursive primality certificate, a probable-prime run and a learned
cost observation retain their own result types.

This chapter is Codex editorial synthesis of completed records in the
Sean Brady/OpenAI ChatGPT/Codex research collaboration. New support
reductions, arithmetic plans and exact implementation changes retain the
technical attribution of their original campaigns. Historical installed
branches are named at their executed versions; the current sole global
runtime has its separately maintained selector.

Certified support and shared premises

The native recursive route retains a known certified factor product F of
N − 1. Its sufficient closure condition is
F ∣ N − 1,   F² > N
The certificate also retains the required exact modular witnesses and
recursively certified prime factors. A checked child can participate in
many parents without being re-created each time. Complete canonical
contents identify a reusable subtree; changed contents require
validation.

This gives different forms of useful reuse. Reference validation
consumes evidence already checked in the current session or a verified
restored checkpoint. Batch validation shares child subtrees and repeated
powers across its admitted targets. Fresh construction searches and
certifies missing support. The installed native service records which of
those operations occurred, together with actual arithmetic and reuse
counters. [V3A10] [V3A11]

For M1279, first native certificate validation takes 0.103482 seconds,
including translation of the historical reachable 61-node inventory into
the newer schema. Warm reference validation has median 0.875
milliseconds. The original construction took 356.585453839 seconds. A
distinct bounded fresh attempt took 0.212750 seconds, retained
F = 524286, and returned KEEP_LOOKING at the order-142 component
boundary. These results identify where the work was saved: validated
evidence makes a strong reusable input, while fresh missing factor
support remains an actual construction task. [V3A10]

The certified progress channel addresses F, keeping unknown N as an
exact integer. Multiplying certified support by c changes its exact
logarithmic margin by
Δlog (F²/N) = 2log c
Those increments have unequal mathematical gain and measured cost. The

Chapter XXIV Prime Event Computation and Microscopic Reliability

The prime-event program constructed a seven-spin stochastic decision
gate inside an event-created sieve and improved its reliability by
changing specified microscopic interactions. The original gate produced
an exact prime/composite decision sequence through one billion for one
of 64 seeds per accumulation profile. A successor selected from the
source algebra before its trajectories were generated produced exact
sequences for 47 of the same 64 seeds per profile. With that source then
frozen, 13 of 16 fresh seeds also produced exact sequences through one
billion. The matched total fell from 569 to 40 errors across the two
paired profiles, a 92.9701 percent reduction. These are completed
experiments with retained source, trajectory, scoring and archive
records. [A2, Sections II-III; PE2]

The chapter follows the construction that made this improvement
intelligible. An accepted event writes future return channels. Two banks
turn the channel history into gate inputs. Six hidden spins provide
controllable pair and triple terms for an output spin. Full finite-state
dynamics carries the hidden configuration from one reading to the next.
Local replay identified the input sector responsible for the baseline’s
first composite errors; elimination of the hidden spins explained that
sector; a balance equation selected the revised couplings. The resulting
observations can then be compared with numerical survival calculations
and stated resource budgets.

The event created sieve

A candidate is an integer n, processed sequentially from 2 to a declared
horizon N. A return channel is a stored period established by an earlier
accepted candidate. If p is accepted, its channel returns at 2p, 3p and
subsequent positive multiples within the horizon. Channels enter two
banks alternately in creation order, beginning with bank 0. A bank
reports whether at least one of its channels returns at the current
candidate. A logical input r_i equals +1 when bank i returns and -1
otherwise.

The ideal decision accepts exactly when neither bank returns. This
recurrence generates the primes by induction. Initially 2 is accepted.
If all accepted candidates below n are precisely the earlier primes,
then a prime n has no earlier prime divisor and receives no return. A
composite n has an earlier prime divisor, whose installed channel
returns at n. Thus the ideal rule accepts every prime and rejects every
composite. The arithmetic resides in integer periods and their future
multiples; the seven-spin system implements the decision from the two
bank inputs. An independent sieve supplies reference labels for scoring
the generated sequence. [A2, Section IIA; PE4, Section 3]

A stochastic decision changes the history. A falsely accepted composite
installs its own channel, and an omitted prime fails to install one.
Either changes the parity of the number of accepted events and can
therefore alter the bank assignment of later channels. Hidden
microscopic state also carries between candidates. An exact trajectory
means no incorrect acceptance or rejection anywhere through N, not
merely a final event count that happens to match. At N=10^9, an exact
trajectory emits all 50,847,534 primes and no composites.

The microscopic gate and its effective margins

The gate has an output spin e and six hidden spins h_j, each taking
values -1 or +1. The two bank inputs are clamped during a candidate. An
accepting reading has e=-1. The source is specified at dimensionless
inverse temperature beta=1 by pair couplings to the hidden spins and
explicit hidden fields. Let x=(e,r_1,r_2). Its energy is

$$E(e,h\mid r_1,r_2)=-\sum_{j=1}^{6}h_j[(Wx)_j+\eta_j]$$

Two hidden spins form pair modules of strength u, with rows (u,u,0) and
(u,0,u). Four more form a triple module of strength t, with rows
(-t,-t,t), (-t,t,-t), (t,-t,-t), (t,t,t). The hidden fields are
eta=(0,0,t,t,t,t). Every microscopic interaction is a pair interaction
or a declared one-spin field. The effective three-spin term appears
after the hidden variables are summed. [A2, Section IIB; PE4, Sections
4-5]

Because the hidden spins are conditionally independent at fixed e and
inputs, their partition factors multiply. The conditional free energy is
therefore

F(e ∣ r₁, r₂) = −∑_(j)log {2cosh [(Wx)_(j) + η_(j)]} = C(r₁, r₂) + eH(r₁, r₂)

where C contains terms independent of the output. The output field and
its two coefficients are

$$H=-a(r_1+r_2)+br_1r_2,\qquad a(u)=\tfrac12\log\cosh(2u)$$

$$b(t)=\tfrac12\log\!\left[\frac{\cosh(2t)^4}{\cosh(4t)}\right]$$

The equilibrium probability of an accepting output is 1/(1+exp(-2H)).
With no returns, H=2a+b and the gate favors acceptance. With one
returning bank, the two pair contributions cancel and H=-b. With both
banks returning, H=b-2a. The positive magnitudes b and 2a-b are the two
composite-blocking margins. They identify how strongly the source favors
rejection in the two composite input sectors.

For the baseline u=4 and t=2, the coefficients are a=3.653426466 and
b=2.960949986. The one-bank margin is 2.960949986; the two-bank margin
is 4.345902946. The baseline consequently gives substantially weaker
static protection to the one-bank sector. This is a source calculation,
not an explanation inferred only from aggregate error counts.

Carrying microscopic state through the decision

The experiment retains all 2^7=128 free-spin configurations. A heat-bath
generator Q_0 assigns a transition rate 1/(1+exp[E(z)-E(s)]) when z
differs from s by one spin flip, with diagonal entries chosen so each
generator column sums to zero. The attempt-rate multiplier is Gamma=512.
A candidate’s total exposure tau_k is split among r confirming readings,
giving the transition matrix T=exp(Q_0 Gamma tau_k/r). Original
production uses r=3.

Every reading samples the complete microscopic state. Its output must
equal -1, and all three readings must accept before the candidate is
emitted. If D projects onto states with e=-1 and v_s is the incoming
state vector, the acceptance probability is

p_(accept)(s) = 1^(T)(DT)^(r)v_(s)

The final sampled state initializes the next candidate whether the
candidate was accepted or rejected. All prescribed random readings are
consumed even after an earlier reading rejects. This preserves both
state history and random-stream alignment. Cubing the equilibrium
acceptance probability would discard finite relaxation and correlations
between readings; it is not the production calculation. [A2, Section
IIC]

Two accumulation profiles use a common 35-phase duration sequence. The
uniform profile has accumulation 1/4, and the rough profile varies its
accumulation over the phases. Exposure is proportional to duration times
the square root of one minus accumulation, with normalization matching
total exposure per cycle. These are supplied dimensionless cadence
profiles. Each seed uses shared random streams across profiles, so the
profiles are paired observations of that seed.

Replay located a correctable microscopic weakness

Baseline seed 1000063 was exact through one billion under both profiles.
The mechanism study compared each other seed’s first incorrect decision
with that seed at the same candidate. Because neither path had yet
erred, the return histories and bank inputs were identical. Across 126
local comparisons, replacing the failing seed’s three draws with the
successful seed’s draws corrected all 126 decisions while retaining the
failing incoming state. Replacing the incoming state while keeping the
failing draws left all 126 decisions incorrect. In 32 comparisons the
incoming states already matched. The realized random triple was the
local discriminator in these comparisons. [A2, Section IIIA; PE4,
Section 9]

Of those first errors, 120 were composite emissions and six were missed
primes. Every first composite emission had exactly one bank returning.
This links the replay directly to the source field: the pair terms
cancel in precisely that sector, leaving b as the blocking margin. The
replay covered 230 saved chunks, 1.15 billion decisions and 264 local
windows, checking replayed event bits and checksums. It resolved the
local first-error mechanisms; it did not generate entire altered futures
after each hypothetical intervention.

One missed-prime example makes the meaning of microscopic replay
explicit. At prime 3,510,937, the failing seed and successful seed both
enter state 103. The failing readings are 56,56,101; the successful
readings are 56,56,56. The third failing draw lies in state 101’s narrow
rejecting interval, about 3.77×10^-11 wide. This is an identified
numerical event in a retained dynamics, rather than an unexplained
exception assigned to a special seed property.

The algebraically chosen successor

At fixed a>0, the smaller of the two blocking margins obeys
min(b,2a-b)<=a, with equality precisely when b=a. The successor
therefore kept the pair-module strength u=4 and raised the four triple
rows and their hidden fields together until b(t)=a. This choice was
fixed before successor trajectories were examined.

Let C=cosh8 and z=cosh(2t)^2. The balance equation becomes z^2=C(2z-1).
Its physical positive-strength root gives

$$t_* =\tfrac12\operatorname{arcosh}\sqrt{C+\sqrt{C^2-C}}=2.3464897298984759\ldots$$

The resulting a=b=3.6534264659876117 in the archived binary64 source
output. Pair strength, temperature, cadence, three-reading rule,
scheduler and matched random streams remain fixed. The triple strength
and associated hidden fields increase by 17.32 percent. This establishes
a maximum of the weaker static margin at fixed pair coefficient. The
subsequent experiment determines the effect on full trajectories. [PE3]

The finite-state calculation shows both sides of the change. The
one-bank three-reading false-emission probability falls from about
1.91×10^-8 to about 3.0×10^-10, approximately a factor of 63. The
two-bank probability rises from 4.74×10^-12 to about 3.01×10^-10,
approximately a factor of 64. The no-return sector also becomes more
reliable. Equalizing the blocking margins transfers protection from the
stronger composite sector to the weaker one; the frequency of those
sectors matters to sequence survival. [A2, Table I]

All 64 seeds 1000000-1000063 were evaluated under both accumulation
profiles using frozen baseline records for the matched comparison. Every
successor trajectory completed through one billion. The final observed
results are:

Per matched profile, 62 seeds improved, one was unchanged, and one
worsened. All balanced matched seeds were exact through 10⁸; the worst
had two errors at 10⁹. The complete matched error count fell from 569 to
40 across profiles. Balanced uniform and rough event sequences agreed
for every matched seed pair. The profiles remain paired because they
share streams; they do not turn 64 seeds into 128 independent seeds.
[PE2]

The fresh continuation fixed the successor source and used all 16 seeds
10000000-10000015 with disjoint base and auxiliary random-seed ranges.
There was no source retuning or seed screening. Thirteen of 16 were
exact through one billion per profile; each of the other three had one
error. The errors were composite emissions at 82,866,449 and 203,033,716
and a missed prime at 997,044,317. Fifteen of 16 were exact through 10⁸.
These fresh streams test the frozen source beyond the matched roster;

The significance is a completed progression from mechanism to source
design to observed improvement. It does not rest on a selected
exceptionally long successful seed: the matched roster and fresh roster
are reported in full, with per-seed outcomes retained. The experiment
measures stochastic sequence reliability in a constructed sieve. Its
exact finite realizations do not issue arbitrary-input primality
certificates or establish an unbounded guarantee; the prime-event
sources do not establish a relation to FEOP.

How retained history changes a later input

A baseline profile pair exposes a concrete feedback path. Rough seed
1000053 accepts an extra composite at 312,596,611. That changes
event-count parity. When prime 483,920,363 is later accepted, its
channel enters a different bank than in the uniform partner. At
967,840,726=2×483,920,363, its return meets the period-2 return. The
partners now present one-bank and two-bank inputs and make different
decisions. The earlier erroneous period does not divide the later
candidate; its influence passes through channel creation order and bank
assignment. [A2, Section IIID; PE4, Section 10]

This example establishes a history-mediated change in a later gate input
and decision. The average effect of post-error feedback on the ensemble
error count is a separate quantity. Zero-error survival ends at the
first error, before post-error amplification can occur. That distinction
permits a clean survival calculation along the ideal input history
without pretending that all later erroneous histories are identical.

Statistical and state aware explanations of the reliability scale

For a Poisson reference with mean error count lambda, the zero-error
probability is exp(-lambda). The baseline uniform mean is 284/64=4.4375,
giving 0.011825, compared with observed 1/64=0.015625. The matched
balanced mean is 20/64=0.3125, giving 0.731616, compared with
47/64=0.734375. The balanced histogram has 47 zero-error, 14 one-error
and three two-error trajectories. Fitted Poisson expectations for these
counts are approximately 46.82,14.63 and 2.29. The sample Fano factors,
variance divided by mean, are 0.9721 for uniform baseline, 0.9619 for
rough baseline and 1.0032 for matched balanced. [A2, Section IVA and
Table III]

These fitted comparisons describe the archived counts. A separate
calculation in the attached expanded analysis predicts survival without
fitting those counts. Along an error-free history, the ideal sieve
determines each input. The microscopic correct-decision operator is
A_n=(DT_n)^r for primes and T_n^r-A_n for composites. Starting from the
no-return Gibbs vector, the product of these substochastic operators
gives the model’s zero-error probability: incorrect paths are removed at
their first wrong decision.

The analysis does not multiply one billion matrices directly. It
calculates the minimum and maximum wrong-decision probabilities over all
128 incoming states for each input and cadence phase, then counts how
often each phase/input combination occurs on the ideal history. Every
conditional distribution given survival lies within those extrema.
Products of their complementary probabilities bound survival without
assuming independent successive decisions. Numerical implementation uses
logarithms of 1-epsilon, with binary64 propagators.

The ideal-history census contains 50,847,534 no-return candidates,
129,835,417 bank-0-only candidates, 77,222,775 bank-1-only candidates
and 742,094,273 both-bank candidates, totaling 999,999,999 decisions.
The resulting uniform-profile numerical survival envelopes are
[0.0158014,0.0158362] for baseline and [0.717107,0.717210] for balanced.
The latter predicts about 45.90 exact cases out of 64 and 11.47 out of
16, compatible with the observed 47 and 13. This is a model-based
explanation of the recorded reliability scale. The quoted envelopes
cover incoming-state variation in the numerical model; they are not
directed-interval certificates of exact real matrix exponentials. [A2,
Section IVB-C and Table IV]

The census also explains the cost of weakening the two-bank margin:
both-bank candidates are the largest sector. Their balanced contribution
is about 0.224 of total hazard 0.332. A useful trajectory objective
therefore weights local error risks by their actual opportunities,
rather than maximizing the weakest static margin alone.

Resource comparisons make the design conclusion precise

Source strength must be defined before alternatives are compared.
Summing absolute couplings gives B_J=4u+12t. Including the four hidden
fields gives B_Jeta=4u+16t. Baseline budgets are (40,48); balanced
budgets are (44.157877,53.543836), increases of 10.39 and 11.55 percent.
The 17.32 percent figure refers specifically to t. These are
dimensionless source-strength conventions; no device energy consumption
was measured.

The attached analysis compares uniform strengthening of the original
u:t=2:1 ratio with balancing under these stated budgets. It also tests
four or five confirming readings while dividing the same candidate
exposure among them. The following are numerical predictions for the
uniform profile, separate from the observed matched and fresh ensembles.
[A2, Section V and Table V]

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Control                   Confirming readings      Predicted zero error
                                                  probability through 10⁹
  ------------------- ------------------------- -------------------------
  Original baseline                           3         0.015801-0.015836

  Original balanced                           3         0.717107-0.717210

  Uniformly scaled                            3         0.708104-0.708408
